### Linux test suite and installer QA (`3a1627fa14e7424d74be2a2d33f9d82de8f1021b`; 2026-11-03)

In an isolated Debian Bookworm `node:24` container cloned from this exact GitHub branch commit, `npm ci` found 0 known vulnerabilities and `npm run test:all` passed: core 657 total (655 passed, 0 failed, 2 Windows-only skips); UI 48/48. `npm ci --omit=dev && npm run build:linux` created all four unsigned QA installers; each SHA-256 sidecar matched its package. Package SHA-256: x64 tar.gz `49837f9e12369314670962c2a2274ca9f6cc193174a4cfceeff9e012a280a41b`; ARM64 tar.gz `946075af9b698bfe52f7c2f567a935712ecad067d230174f08fb933075a60e9c`; amd64 `.deb` `e06134e8b052956701c7cc5b55881da820808d767c3e4e95477a9a10f7ed8aed`; arm64 `.deb` `57731c5cb87ff70a918d4c200bdf4b3f241229e85c0bfe6d44d3c5d3ade8098f`.

The staged x64 runtime passed multilingual OCR, scanned-PDF OCR, safe HTML, MCP startup and a private-note canary check. The amd64 Debian package installed, reported version 1.2.8, initialized a fictional vault and removed cleanly. The x64 per-user tar installer installed, initialized a fictional vault and uninstalled while preserving the vault and settings. The arm64 Debian package installed, reported 1.2.8, initialized a fictional vault and removed cleanly. Under Docker ARM emulation, the ARM64 tar runtime passed multilingual OCR, scanned-PDF OCR, safe HTML, MCP and private-note canary checks. These are container QA artifacts only: no package was published, graphical Linux desktop behavior and physical ARM hardware remain untested, and ARM emulation is not native hardware evidence.

### Current Windows installed-app smoke (`0e86ffd551bf8260ff0a56e393ca9249d93961df`; 2026-11-03)

`npm run smoke:installed:win` rebuilt the unsigned Windows 1.2.8 QA installer from the current branch and passed. The installed app launched from its bundled Node runtime; both shortcuts opened Setup; folder-ingestion controls loaded; synthetic English, Portuguese and Traditional Chinese image OCR passed; scanned-PDF OCR passed; HTML extraction omitted scripts and hidden content; conflict retry did not duplicate the saved note; the temporary Hermes hook was preserved; the isolated sign-in launcher passed health and removed only its own entry; and uninstall removed only the QA app while preserving test data and restoring PATH. This is synthetic local Windows evidence. The package is unsigned and unpublished; it does not prove Mac/Linux behavior, user acceptance, or production readiness.

### Local reranker RSI experiment (commit `08c2732`; 2026-11-03)

Added a third fixed RSI experiment, visible only when the user has enabled the local loopback reranker. It runs on the bundled 21-case fictional vault, sends only synthetic queries and already-authorized synthetic snippets to that local service, and aborts if the reranker fails instead of scoring fallback search. A candidate with lower hit@k or reciprocal rank is marked `quality_regression`; only a non-regressing candidate can be eligible for human review. It never changes live search settings or promotes itself. This small development set is not held-out or independent model-quality evidence, and no token savings or real-model gain is claimed.

Windows / Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed on the working tree: core 657 total (647 passed, 0 failed, 10 platform/privilege skips); UI 48/48 passed. Focused reranker/RSI/privacy tests passed as part of the full run. No real reranker service or model was used. GitHub Actions remain intentionally unused; this is local test evidence only.

### Real Windows Ollama inference check (source commit `efb42bbd7616ab4d2c641a5926c9ebefa97eb40f`; 2026-11-03)

Installed the signed per-user Ollama Windows app and pulled `qwen3.5:2b` (2.7 GB; model digest `0689d44085e06d165161a8a9a1731344278cfb5aade63a3c3dbdb48ab54b130a`). Windows 11 Pro, Node.js 24.22.1, NVIDIA RTX 2060 with 4 GB reported VRAM, driver `32.0.15.8142`. The model reported `completion`, `vision`, `tools`, and `thinking` capabilities and loaded onto the GPU.

BrainLink `generateLocalSessionSummary` completed on a fictional Maple Desk session in 132 seconds and returned a structured, source-bounded summary with the expected goal, draft outcome, decision, and next action. It also added two plausible but unsupported open questions; therefore the output correctly remains `local_model_unverified` and is not quality evidence. A direct loopback Ollama JSON request also succeeded. `generateLocalIngestionDraft` completed on fictional text in 3.2 seconds after model warm-up and produced a source-linked `unverified_draft`. These checks establish that local text inference works through the current BrainLink code on this computer, but performance is slow on this GPU.

The synthetic PNG vision-draft check did not pass: Ollama reported vision capability, but the generated response omitted the required `visual_description`, so BrainLink rejected it without saving an incomplete draft. Image understanding is therefore not verified for this model and path. No real vault or personal content was used. Focused automated local-summary, ingestion-draft, and local-summary-setup tests passed 16/16 on the same checkout. The attempted name-filtered full-suite command was mis-scoped and one test file failed at file level; it was stopped, and the focused test files were rerun directly and passed. No application source code changed in this check. Hosted CI, different hardware, image quality, and customer acceptance remain unverified.

### Corrective retry for incomplete local vision drafts (`50711ce`, 2026-11-03)

When a vision-capable Ollama model returned the rest of a draft but omitted `visual_description`, BrainLink now makes one local-only follow-up request for that missing field and merges it into the first response. It still refuses to save if the description is absent after the retry. A live synthetic OCR fixture check using `qwen3.5:2b` succeeded in 3.5 seconds after warm-up; it returned the visible text description and remained an `unverified_draft`. This is one image, not broad visual-quality evidence. The focused ingestion draft tests passed 12/12. The full Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed on source commit `50711ce`: core 647 total (637 passed, 0 failed, 10 Windows privilege/platform skips); UI 48/48. A previous full attempt had one transient file-level failure in `test/brainlink.test.mjs`; its standalone rerun and the clean full rerun both passed. No real vault or personal image was used. Live image quality across other models and customer acceptance remain unverified.

### Longer local-model deadline for slower devices (`f6afd66`, 2026-11-03)

Raised the shared local Ollama request deadline from two minutes to five minutes for session summaries and source drafts. This follows the observed 132-second fictional session-summary run on the RTX 2060; a two-minute limit would abort that otherwise successful operation. The deadline remains bounded, and the Sessions hint now warns in English, Portuguese, and Traditional Chinese that slower computers may take a few minutes. Focused local-summary, ingestion-draft and local-summary UI tests passed 22/22; all three locale JSON files parsed and `git diff --check` passed. The full suite passed immediately before this small timeout/localized-copy change on `50711ce`; a full-suite rerun on `f6afd66` has not been performed. No live inference was rerun after changing the timeout value.

The Sources panel now shows a clear message when scheduled folder scans are enabled but BrainLink's quiet start-at-sign-in option is off. The message links directly to the Background setting. These are local synthetic-data results; sign-in behavior on a user's account, native macOS/Linux desktop behavior, hosted CI, signing, public distribution and customer acceptance remain unverified.

### Vault note count timing fix (working tree based on `32fe33b`, 2026-11-03)

Setup now sends completed vault note counts to browsers that connect after the background scan, and buffers early count events until vault state loads. This addresses a visible race where the page could stay on “Counting notes…” even though the count was ready. Removed a duplicate `/api/events` handler. Focused tests passed 4/4. A first full Windows suite run had two non-reproducing failures; both passed individually. A clean rerun on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 passed: core 638 total (628 passed, 0 failed, 10 platform/privilege skips), UI 44/44. Browser visual recheck was not completed because the computer-use session stopped at its URL safety check.

After that test run, `npm run smoke:installed:win` rebuilt and installed the current 1.2.8 Windows package in an isolated test profile. Bundled-runtime startup, both shortcuts, Setup and folder-ingestion controls, English/Portuguese/Traditional Chinese OCR, scanned-PDF OCR, safe HTML extraction, retry-safe filing, temporary Hermes-hook setup/cleanup, uninstall, test-home preservation, and PATH restoration all passed. This is an unsigned local installer check; it does not establish signing or a public release.

### Verified update fallback recovery (working tree based on `626bca5e`, 2026-11-02)

The update screen now keeps its manual download link after the user opens the verified installer. A user can choose one installer-open action and one manual download, in either order; each action rechecks the staged file's path, size, and SHA-256. A completed first action extends the staged ticket lifetime to 24 hours; an unused ticket still expires after five minutes. Setup prevents duplicate or overlapping actions and removes temporary files at expiry. The status copy is localized in English, Portuguese, and Traditional Chinese. On Windows/Node.js 24.22.1, focused updater API tests passed 2/2, including overlapping open/download actions. The full `npm run test:all` passed: core 636 total (626 passed, 0 failed, 10 platform/privilege skips); UI 44/44. The isolated unsigned Windows installer smoke also passed on this code, covering bundled-runtime launch, shortcuts, Setup/folder scan controls, OCR in three languages, scanned PDF and HTML ingestion, retry-safe filing, temporary Hermes hook enable/disable, and isolated uninstall/data/PATH preservation. ### Signed-update key rotation overlap support (working tree based on `7c570e0`, 2026-11-02)

### Source-backed entity proposals in Raw-to-Wiki (current working tree)

When local Ollama creates an intake draft, it may suggest up to eight entities from BrainLink's fixed type list. Each candidate must include an exact quote found in the redacted source text or it is discarded. It can also suggest up to twelve typed relationships, but only among unique, current records in the same vault whose titles appear in the selected source. BrainLink checks the quoted evidence, allowed predicate and matching scope; a person selects proposals and confirms saving. Entity notes stay `status: proposed`; relation entries stay `review: proposed`, with a wikilink from the existing subject note to the source. Existing graph review controls remain the separate approval step. Scheduled auto-file does not create entity or relation proposals. If a selected suggestion fails after the main note is saved, BrainLink keeps a private progress record and offers a retry in the original destination; it skips completed suggestions and records the source revision as filed only after all selected suggestions finish. A conflict-and-retry fixture confirms the existing file is preserved and the main note is not duplicated. On Windows/Node.js 24.22.1, focused ingestion/schedule tests pass 17/17 and the full `npm run test:all` passes: core 634 total (624 passed, 0 failed, 10 platform/privilege skips), UI 44/44. Live Ollama quality, generated-answer quality and visual browser acceptance remain unverified. Automatic extraction from sessions or arbitrary unsourced context remains future work.

### Recover partial Raw-to-Wiki filing (working tree based on `9331ddb`, 2026-11-02)

The main proposed note and its optional entity/relationship proposals no longer lose the retry path when only some selected suggestions save. A validated, private local progress record stores the fixed destination and selected suggestions; each successful entity or relation is recorded. On retry, BrainLink revalidates the exact source and destination, reuses the saved main-note path, and skips completed suggestions. If an interruption occurred after an entity or relationship reached disk but before its progress receipt, BrainLink recognizes the matching source-backed proposed record and does not create a duplicate. The ingestion receipt is written only after all selected suggestions are complete. Setup shows the saved note path and a retry action. A fictional conflict test verifies preservation of the unrelated file, no duplicate main note, and successful completion after retry. Focused ingestion/schedule tests passed 17/17; the full Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 suite passed core 634 total (624 passed, 0 failed, 10 platform/privilege skips) and UI 44/44. This is local automated evidence; live Ollama quality, hosted CI and customer acceptance remain unverified.

### Twin memory labels on saved notes (current working tree)

Twin-scoped note retrieval now treats an unclassified note as semantic and refuses unknown explicit `memory_type` labels. The note proposal API validates optional `memory_type`, writes it as frontmatter for both vault schemas, and refuses a scoped agent's proposal when that memory type is disabled. Raw-to-Wiki filing now saves the generated category in `memory_type` while keeping the separate note `type` as `note`. Fictional regression coverage checks malformed and untyped notes, disabled memory access, and saved ingestion metadata. ### Profile-scoped automatic retrieval feedback (current working tree)

Successful `vault_search` and `context_packet` calls from a named, profile-scoped AI now add content-free retrieval observations to the existing RSI ledger. The record contains only the Twin profile ID, tool, search verdict, timestamp, and a coarse response-time bucket. It never records the question, question hash, vault name, note path, excerpt, or answer; legacy and unassigned connections are not recorded. `brainlink miss observations --profile PROFILE_ID` reports profile-separated aggregates. The optional scheduled and manual RSI review compares each profile's current and previous 30-day weak-match rates, requires at least 20 current searches, and can open a fixed review-only proposal when the rate is high or sharply rising. It does not label correct abstentions as errors or change ranking/content. On Windows/Node.js 24.22.1, full `npm run test:all` passed on this working tree: core 629 total (621 passed, 0 failed, 8 platform/privilege skips); UI 44/44. ### Local HTML and HTM ingestion (2026-11-02; current working tree based on `bda0276`)

The selected-folder scanner now queues `.html` and `.htm` sources and extracts the title, visible body text, and image alt text using the pinned `parse5` HTML parser inside a worker with a 15-second deadline and 96 MB old-generation memory cap. It does not execute page scripts or fetch external resources. Script, style, template, SVG/canvas, iframe/object/embed, form controls, and explicit hidden/ARIA-hidden/inline-hidden nodes are omitted. ### Excel and PowerPoint text support for selected-folder ingestion (working tree based on `34c0082`, 2026-11-02)

Added bounded local text extraction for `.xlsx` and `.pptx` to the existing selected-folder intake. Excel extraction follows workbook relationships and omits hidden sheets, rows, and columns. PowerPoint extraction follows the visible slide list and omits hidden slides; speaker notes and embedded media are not extracted. Office ZIP structure is checked for traversal, duplicates, encryption, oversized entries, and expansion limits; XML DTD/entity declarations are refused. ### Local image text OCR for selected-folder ingestion (code commit `404e602`, 2026-11-02)

Added PNG/JPEG/BMP intake with local OCR. BrainLink validates the file signature and image dimensions before handing text extraction to pinned Tesseract.js; images are capped at 24 million pixels and 10,000 pixels per side. English, Portuguese, and Traditional Chinese best-integer traineddata are shipped as compressed local assets (about 6.0 MB total), so the native app does not fetch model files at runtime. OCR uses a 60-second deadline and returns at most 24,000 characters; it extracts text and does not describe image scenes. The selected-folder permission, exclusion, exact-source-revision, proposal, and auto-file rules remain the same. ### Background folder re-check mode (code commit `5eca915`, 2026-11-02)

Added an opt-in repeated polling mode to the existing selected-folder workflow. Users can choose daily scanning or re-check selected folders every 1, 5, 15, 30, or 60 minutes, while keeping the manual “Scan now” action. Polling runs while BrainLink is open; the existing opt-in sign-in launcher can reopen it quietly after login. It does not install an OS watcher or run as a Windows service. It reuses the current scoped scan, local Ollama draft, review, and optional same-vault auto-file pipeline. Drafting rechecks current enablement and selected targets before sending source text to the local model, so turning the schedule off or changing scope stops stale candidates from being drafted. A neighboring unselected folder was planted as a privacy canary and remained unread. On Windows/Node.js 24.22.1, exact commit `5eca915` passed `npm run test:all`: core 619 total (611 passed, 0 failed, 8 platform/privilege skips); UI 44/44. ### Raw-to-Wiki automatic filing work (2026-11-02)

Started branch `feat/complete-raw-wiki-folder-flow` from the freshly fetched `origin/main` commit `8336f10`. Added an opt-in automatic filing setting, per-scan-folder destination selection, and code to save local Ollama drafts as new linked `proposed` notes after scheduled or manual scans. The source folder and destination must be in the same non-private vault; existing notes are never overwritten. Scans support Markdown, TXT, CSV/TSV, JSON, XML, YAML and LOG; HTML, PDFs, Office files, images and other formats are skipped pending separate extraction support. Up to five local AI drafts can be made per run, and the schedule still requires BrainLink to be open. Non-Markdown formats do not have Markdown frontmatter privacy flags; their boundary is the explicitly selected non-private vault/folder plus path exclusions. A dedicated agent read tool accepts only a queued source ID and exact revision, and is hidden unless the profile grants `read_note`. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed on this source: core 617 total (609 passed, 0 failed, 8 platform/privilege skips), UI 44/44. ### Diagnose stale Claude Desktop processes after BrainLink setup changes (pre-commit working tree based on `eb08162`, 2026-10-31)

The Windows Setup health check now compares Claude Desktop process start times with the saved Claude MCP configuration's modification time. If the oldest Claude process predates that configuration, the message says Claude was already running when the settings changed and asks the user to fully quit it, including the tray icon, then reopen and start a new chat. The check returns only a small status (`running` and whether it predates the config); it does not expose process IDs or timestamps, inspect conversation data, restart Claude, or claim that Claude loaded or called BrainLink. Non-Windows systems and inspection failures report unknown and keep the existing general instruction. This is a diagnostic hint because some client versions may reload MCP settings while running.

The live local Windows check on Ian's setup returned `started_before_config: true`, matching the reported connection problem. If no Claude process is found, Setup says to open Claude; if process inspection fails, it falls back to the general restart instruction. ### Preserve restore recovery paths when publication or rollback fails (code commit `28cfafd`, 2026-10-31)

Restore still writes and verifies a complete staged copy before publishing it into the user's empty destination. If the final rename fails, BrainLink now explicitly attempts to return the original empty destination. If that rollback also fails, the error reports the exact sibling path where the original folder remains. If temporary recovered-copy cleanup fails, the error also reports that path instead of silently hiding the leftover. Added deterministic rename-failure tests with fictional data: publishing failure returns the original destination and cleans staging; a second rollback failure preserves the original folder and reports its location. On exact source commit `28cfafd`, Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 602 total (594 passed, 0 failed, 8 Windows platform/privilege skips), UI 44/44. ### Prevent scheduled scans from restoring a now-private vault target (commit `107b1e0`, 2026-10-31)

The background scan timer and Setup routes that change a vault's privacy or the scan target list now serialize their config updates. Vault removal and scheduled-scan settings use the same update queue.

### Do not publish partial session imports while a source is changing (commit `310f3d3`, 2026-10-30)

Claude Code, Codex, and DeepSeek Harness importers now compare source metadata before and after reading. If an active transcript changes during the read, BrainLink defers it without storing a partial session or advancing its cursor; the next scan retries the complete transcript. After storage, the importer checks again and keeps the cursor behind if the source changed during that step. Cursor files are replaced atomically, including for Hermes, so an interrupted write cannot leave truncated JSON.

Verification on Windows with Node.js 24.22.1: `test/sessions.test.mjs` passed 24/24; the formerly timing-sensitive RSI discovery test passed alone and in the full run; `npm run test:all` passed with core 599 total (591 passed, 0 failed, 8 Windows platform/privilege skips) and UI 44/44. `git diff --check` passed. Tests used fictional transcripts; live vendor clients were not writing transcripts concurrently during this run. The commit is pushed to the integration branch, not merged or released.

The same source commit `310f3d3` also passed `npm run smoke:backup-recovery`: encrypted fictional Personal, Organisation and Client-A data; four files verified and restored; wrong-key refusal; artifact restored; source files unchanged. ### DeepSeek Harness Zstandard compatibility (2026-10-30)

Fixed the local session importer for Harness files made of concatenated Zstandard frames. Node.js 22's built-in decoder returns only the first frame, which dropped later events from a session. BrainLink now checks each frame and block boundary, decompresses frames separately, enforces the existing total output limit, and refuses malformed or truncated frames before importing partial text. The importer still stores only direct user prompts and visible assistant text, redacts secrets, and keeps imported sessions private by default.

### Optional scheduled weekly RSI benchmark (commit `7d04dcd`, 2026-10-30)

Setup can now run the existing fixed 20-case fictional retrieval benchmark automatically once per week. It is **off by default**; the user chooses a weekday and local time. It runs only while BrainLink is open, uses the built-in fictional vault, stores aggregate results and source revision details, and does not read connected vaults or call an AI model. An interrupted run can retry after restart on the scheduled day; a completed failed attempt waits until the next week or a manual run. This measures retrieval task success, not generated-answer quality, provider-billed token savings, or RSI promotion eligibility.

### Privacy-aware RSI retrieval-miss evidence and weekly benchmark (commit `3b75063`, 2026-10-30)

The CLI records misses and runs/reports a fixed 20-case schema-2 benchmark against a fictional vault. A miss requires a Personal/Organisation/Client profile label. The submitted question is checked for secrets and converted to a normalized SHA-256 fingerprint before storage; its wording is not saved. Listing requires an exact profile label. Scores are retrieval task-success proxies, not independent answer-quality proof. At this original commit, miss capture and weekly runs were manual; the weekly schedule was added later.


Setup now includes a manual **Run benchmark** button and history for the same fixed fictional fixture. The authenticated routes do not accept a user vault or fixture path; the operation uses the sample vault shipped with BrainLink and disables semantic-model search. It still reports only retrieval task success and privacy counts; it is not a test of generated-answer quality or a self-improvement promotion gate.

### Open a verified update package from Setup (2026-10-30)

The button is one-use; while BrainLink stays open, the staged installer is retained for up to 24 hours. The manual download link remains available if opening fails. This does not enable unsigned updates or bypass the OS confirmation.

### Enforce Twin source and session-capture policies (2026-10-30)

Twin `source_policy` values now affect runtime behavior. Profile-scoped `propose_note` marks new notes private when `private_default` is enabled and enforces a non-empty source, stable task/session ID, and supported confidence label when `provenance_required` is enabled. `metadata-only` profiles cannot log or close session content; `summary-proposal` allows private summaries but blocks full-text reads; `full-text-opt-in` enables full-text access only when the profile selects it, with the separate BrainLink “share full text” choice still required for imported private sessions. The policy is returned to the agent in `brainlink_guide` and reflected in conditional tool requirements.

Synthetic tests verify the policy both at tool-list and direct-call boundaries, that note previews carry the private flag and provenance, that metadata-only direct writes are refused, and that summary/full-text modes admit only their intended data. On Windows with Node.js 24.22.1, `npm run test:all` passed: core 581 total (573 passed, 0 failed, 8 platform/privilege skips); UI 44/44. Focused Twin/session tests passed 51/51. `git diff --check` passed. Tests use fictional vaults; no customer note content was changed. This is local automated evidence, not hosted CI or live cross-client acceptance.

### Keep public RSI fingerprints out of generic secret false positives (2026-10-30)

`findSecrets` now exempts only exact-length public hashes in `fixture_sha256` and `source_commit_sha`. Long hex strings in ordinary text and key-shaped or malformed values in those fields still trigger the scanner. Focused BrainLink and RSI-ledger tests passed 92/94 (2 Windows symlink-privilege skips). One full Windows run had a transient `EPERM` during RSI discovery settings replacement; that test passed alone and on the complete rerun. Final `npm run test:all` passed: core 581 total (573 passed, 0 failed, 8 platform/privilege skips); UI 44/44. The four focused tests in draft PR #4 also pass on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 when run in a disposable worktree with this scanner fix applied. This does not validate the full contributor branch or integrate it; the PR remains draft.

### Claude Desktop Anna activation on a large vault (2026-10-30)

Claude's first explicit `agent_connect` call timed out at the client's 60-second limit. The delay came from reading one known note through `readNote`, which built the full Brain vault index first, and from `agent_connect` building whole-vault recent activity/open-loop summaries on every activation. Commit `73ea027efb584d13c1140b749df6163d36f89af5` removes the full index build from direct note reads while preserving path containment, excluded-folder, symlink, privacy, and memory-type checks. Normal agent activation now reads configured notes only; the full activity summary is opt-in with `include_vault_context=true`.

Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` on commit `73ea027` passed: core 579 total (571 passed, 0 failed, 8 platform/privilege skips) and UI 44/44. Focused note-read/agent-activation tests passed 8/8 with one additional Windows symlink-privilege skip. After a full Claude Desktop process restart, a live Claude call invoked BrainLink `agent_connect` and returned “Connected to Anna in BrainLink (vault: Brain, platform: Claude),” including the sourced Anna role and approval limits. A separate local MCP check completed in 97 ms and confirmed the connection profile exposes only the Brain vault with semantic and procedural memory. This verifies the one configured Claude Desktop profile and local machine; it does not establish performance on other vault sizes or clients.

### Large-vault Setup responsiveness and scoped Claude Desktop connection (2026-10-30)

Setup no longer builds the full search index just to display a vault's note count. It returns immediately, counts only visible Markdown notes from bounded frontmatter reads in the background, and updates the Home and vault-card counts when done. On the configured local Brain vault, the updated Setup loaded in Chrome and displayed its completed count instead of `null notes`; the process stayed near 96 MB during the measured state request, versus about 2.8 GB before the fix. The connection UI was used to assign Claude Desktop to the existing Organisation Twin with semantic and procedural memory and Brain search/read/propose actions. Working, episodic, approved-session, and private-session access remain off. Claude Desktop's local config now contains the scoped token, but the app has not yet been restarted, so an actual Claude tool call is still unverified. No token or note content is recorded here.

Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` on source commit `0fd586c97caba6d8bd4391e518bb5a634cf89826` passed: core 577 total (570 passed, 0 failed, 7 platform/privilege skips); UI 44/44. Focused lazy-count and vault-count tests passed. This is local test and Setup evidence only; it does not verify that Claude Desktop has loaded the server until it is restarted and tested in Claude.

### Recheck memory permissions before reusing cached search notes (2026-10-30)

Profile-scoped retrieval now re-reads the bounded frontmatter before reusing a cached note when file size and timestamps appear unchanged. If the current memory type is not allowed, the cached note is removed before ranking or excerpts. Direct note reads also inspect current frontmatter before loading the body, with a second check after reading for concurrent edits. The regression simulates matching size, modified time and change time, then verifies both search and direct reads deny an episodic-only note to an agent without episodic access. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 575 total (568 passed, 0 failed, 7 platform/privilege skips) and UI 44/44; focused Twin profile tests passed 25/25. Hosted GitHub Actions remain unable to start because of the account billing/spending gate; this is local test evidence only.

### Check memory-type permission before direct note body reads (2026-10-30)

Direct note reads now re-open only the bounded frontmatter before loading the full Markdown body. If the current note type is outside the requesting AI's Twin memory permissions, BrainLink denies the read before invoking the body reader; it still rechecks privacy and memory type after the body read to catch a file changed during the read. A regression reproduces a stale index with preserved size and timestamp and asserts that the body reader is called zero times. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 575 total (568 passed, 0 failed, 7 platform/privilege skips) and UI 44/44. Focused Twin profile tests passed 25/25. Hosted GitHub Actions remain unable to start because of the account billing/spending gate; this is local test evidence only.

### Release update manifest signing tool (2026-10-30)

Added `src/update-signing.mjs`, `scripts/sign-update-manifest.mjs`, and `npm run updates:sign-manifest`. The release tool reads a payload and Ed25519 private key from files, checks the payload with the same verifier shipped in BrainLink, derives the key ID, creates a detached envelope, refuses to overwrite an existing output, and does not log or include private key material. Unix key files must be owner-only; Windows ACL safety is the release operator's responsibility. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 full `npm run test:all` passed on the implementation before final CLI input-size hardening: core 563 total (557 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 43/43. After the final tool changes, both focused updater files passed 8/8 on Windows and in Docker Node 24 / Debian Bookworm; `git diff --check` passed. No production key was created, no public key is pinned into packages, and no in-app update/download/install flow exists yet.

### Scheduled folder scan recovery after app restart (2026-10-30)

Added an API-level regression to the existing fictional-vault scan test: if BrainLink was closed at the configured daily time, opening the background service later that day catches up the scan once; the saved local run date prevents a duplicate scan. Manual scanning, multi-folder scope, private-vault exclusion and content-free activity logging remain covered by the same test. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 focused run: `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/ingestion-schedule.test.mjs test/startup.test.mjs` passed 12/12; `git diff --check` passed. This verifies scheduler recovery in the local service test fixture, not unattended startup on each packaged operating system.

### Four memory types in the desktop Notes view (2026-10-30)

The Notes view now shows the selected note's explicit memory type and offers a plain-language selector for Working, Semantic, Procedural, and Episodic. Unrecognized notes show **Unclassified** and are not automatically guessed. Choosing a type updates only the `memory_type` frontmatter field, keeps other metadata, and uses the existing save/cancel flow. The user must choose **Use suggestion**; arbitrary titles and note text are not classified. The displayed saved type now follows unsaved frontmatter edits immediately. The new tests cover known aliases, explicit-type precedence, unknown metadata, and non-mutating suggestions. Windows/Node.js `npm run test:all`: core 555 total (549 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 33/33; `git diff --check` passed. ### Typed graph relation phrase disambiguation (working tree based on `6ea2fb3`, 2026-10-30)

Relationship discovery previously treated “belongs to” and Portuguese “pertence a” as both ownership and membership. It now maps those phrases to `part_of`; explicit “owned by” / “owner is” remain `owned_by`. Regression tests cover both English rules and the Portuguese membership phrase. Focused Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 graph suites passed 38/38. Full `npm run test:all` also passed: core 551 total (545 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 29/29. Suggestions remain unverified proposals and still need human review. General natural-language relation extraction and live-vault precision are not established.

### Reviewed related-note links in Raw-to-Wiki drafts (working tree based on `4b5539a`, 2026-10-30)

An ingestion draft can now show up to eight related-note suggestions from the same authorized vault; the user may select up to five for inclusion in the proposed note. Suggestions are based on shared terms and are labeled as unverified similarity suggestions, not factual relationships. Private notes are excluded by the existing scope-aware related-note search, and selected paths are checked against both the draft's original candidates and a fresh authorized search before saving. Tests use a synthetic public note and a planted private canary; they verify the private note is not suggested and cannot be added by forging the save request. A small-vault threshold edge case was fixed so a two-note vault can produce suggestions. Verification on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1: focused ingestion tests passed 5/5; `npm run test:all` passed, core 548 total (542 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 29/29. Syntax, locale JSON parsing, and whitespace checks passed. Similarity quality, real-vault usefulness, visual UI acceptance, and token/time savings are not measured. Suggestions do not create approved graph edges.

### Local Raw-to-Wiki AI draft path (working tree based on `7cdafa1`, 2026-10-30)

Setup can prepare a local Ollama draft from one exact Raw-to-Wiki queue item. An additional opt-in lets each daily scan create up to five local drafts; the draft queue persists in private BrainLink app data across restarts, while original source text is not stored there. Before each local model call, BrainLink rechecks vault scope, privacy, and the exact source revision. Input is redacted and bounded; the fixed model endpoint is loopback only; source instructions are treated as untrusted. Each draft suggests one of the four memory types, includes an exact source wikilink, and remains explicitly unverified. Failed local model calls use the scanner's bounded retry schedule; already saved drafts persist and are skipped on retry. The user chooses an existing destination outside the scanned source folder and separately confirms saving; BrainLink writes a `status: proposed`, `confidence: reported` note, then records the filing receipt. Stale, private, excluded, already-reviewed, and generated sources do not reach the model. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 verification on commit `7b37249`: `npm run test:all` passed: core 543 total (537 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. Retry follow-up commit `814478d`: full `npm run test:all` passed: core 544 total (538 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29; focused ingestion draft/schedule tests passed 9/9; `git diff --check` passed. GitHub Actions for `fe29448` failed before runner assignment (`runner_id: 0`, no steps or job logs) on both push and pull_request events; hosted CI has not verified the code. Tests use fictional vaults and a fake local model. Live Ollama, visual usability, provider token/time savings, and independent content quality remain unverified. Drafts are never automatically saved to a vault or promoted to verified knowledge.

### Combined context-packet size limit (2026-10-30)

`context_packet` now accepts `max_total_chars` (80–2,400) to cap the combined excerpt text, not only each excerpt. It returns the requested budget and actual excerpt character count, and reduces the number of cited sources when needed to retain at least 80 characters per source. Default remains up to four sources × 300 characters (1,200 total). This is a character bound, not an exact token measurement. ### Session closeout source verification (2026-10-28)

At source commit `064d3fd70b01ad490f24e4f77594da93b72498e4`, closeout now verifies an optional `source_session_id` before preparing a note. BrainLink reads only the session metadata, applies the same imported-session sharing or exact Twin-profile visibility rules as session reading, and rejects missing or hidden sessions. If the caller also provides a project, it must exactly match the source session's stored project. Rejections happen before either closeout record is written. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed on this source tree: core 532 total (526 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform skips) and UI 29/29. ### Hermes CLI session history import (2026-10-28)

Added Hermes session history to BrainLink’s shared local session index. The importer opens the selected Hermes `state.db` with SQLite read-only mode, reads only ended `cli` sessions and active `user`/`assistant` content, redacts secrets, stores sessions privately, uses an incremental per-profile cursor, and caps imported message/session text. It excludes other sources (including messaging), in-progress sessions, inactive/archived messages, system prompts, tools, and reasoning. Users can select a profile folder from **Settings → Sessions**, or use the default resolved from `HERMES_HOME` / the platform default. BrainLink does not enumerate or merge profile databases. Switching profiles retains previously imported local session history. Unsupported Node versions or database layouts produce a clear import result without opening the source database writable.

On Windows with Node.js 24.22.1, the initial full `npm run test:all` passed on the importer implementation: core 527 total (521 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform-specific skips), UI 29/29. The profile-picker change is covered by focused API/UI tests: selecting a synthetic profile imports only that profile, invalid folders and bad tokens are rejected, resetting returns to the default, and earlier imported sessions remain. The importer tests also cover source-file byte equality before/after import, private defaults and explicit summary/full-text consent, incremental pickup, missing-marker refusal, and sync/MCP wiring. This is local fixture verification; Ian’s Hermes history and live Hermes model use are not verified. Official design references: [Hermes session storage](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/website/docs/developer-guide/session-storage.md) and [Node.js SQLite API](https://nodejs.org/api/sqlite.html).

### Bounded cited context packets (2026-10-28)

Added the read-only `context_packet` MCP tool for query-focused retrieval. It reuses `vault_search` ranking, so vault scope, private-note filtering, and Twin semantic-memory policy run before packet formatting. Agents can cap results at eight sources and each excerpt at 300 characters; the default is four sources with full 300-character excerpts. Results carry exact vault-relative path, title, note date/status, validated confidence when present, and only include historical/truncation flags when applicable. The packet reports `not_in_brain` without inventing content; tool guidance tells agents to use `vault_read_note` for more text. On the frozen 30-case fictional retrieval set, default packets retained task success (17/30), source recall (1.000), and zero forbidden-source hits versus compact `vault_search`; estimated response size fell 27.5% (10,127 vs 13,966 tokens). The regression test covers this comparison, excerpt bounds, citations, empty results, private canaries, and Twin semantic grants. These are local token estimates and a source/context proxy; provider-billed savings and generated-answer quality remain unverified. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 focused run across retrieval, core, profile, and response-metric tests passed 112 tests with 1 platform skip. Full suite has not been rerun after the packet default was tuned.

### Typed graph proposal quality checks (2026-10-28)

Added `brainlink eval-graph --fixture FILE`, a content-free evaluation of deterministic relationship proposals. It reports precision, recall, F1, privacy-canary hits, latency, exact fixture SHA-256, source commit/branch, tree cleanliness and environment. Fewer than 20 labelled source notes returns `needs_more_cases`; results are eligible only for human review and never promote a rule. The report omits note text, paths, titles, IDs and canary values. A 23-case fictional regression set in `test/typed-graph-eval.test.mjs` covers English, Portuguese and Chinese positive statements, unrelated text, target-before-verb order, negation and a foreign-scope target. It caught a false positive where “does not consume” became a proposed edge. The bounded detector now ignores common English, Portuguese and Chinese negation markers before the relation verb and preserves the positive phrase “not only consumes.” On this authored test set, precision and recall are both 1.0 with zero privacy violations. This is a targeted regression check, not an independently authored quality benchmark or proof of general extraction accuracy. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed on the working tree based at `cb18840`: core 520 total (514 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 29/29. Focused graph and evaluator tests passed 19/19; syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed.

### Invalidate cached public notes after privacy-preserving file replacement (2026-10-28)

`loadVault` now includes filesystem change time at nanosecond precision in its cache key, and rechecks the active Twin's note-memory permission before reusing cached text. This prevents a same-size note replacement that preserves modified time from leaving its old public text in retrieval after it becomes private. A regression changes `public: false` to `private: true` without changing byte length, restores the original modified time, verifies the filesystem change time changed, and confirms search removes the cached content and direct read is denied.

Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 verification on exact commit `52594c111fe7516390f30fd73acb1a766560c211`: `npm run test:all` passed after the final cache-check ordering adjustment — core 503 total (497 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 29/29. The six skips include Windows symlink-privilege and non-Windows platform cases. This is automated local evidence; hosted CI, filesystem behavior on other filesystems, and live multi-client acceptance remain unverified.

### DeepSeek Harness turn-closeout suggestion (2026-10-28)

Added an opt-in Cordis plugin for connected named Harness profiles. It tracks root-agent sessions and records a local cue only after a durable `turn/end` event with reason `completed`; it does not treat agent disposal or failed turns as task completion. A new turn clears the prior cue, and Harness cues older than 14 days are hidden. The cue stores only client, session ID, time, and reason; Harness cues do not trigger automatic local summaries. Existing closeout remains preview-first and user-approved.

Setup backs up and merges the selected profile's `cordis.patch.yml`, preserves unrelated plugins, removes only BrainLink's own entry, and refuses shared home-level installation. It verifies the plug-in file before showing the setting as enabled. The Sessions UI warns that Harness plugins run with the Harness process's user permissions. The feature has fictional profile and event tests only; no live Harness profile, installed runtime event, imported real session, or model call was tested. A current full-suite run overlapped the final hardening edits and is not used as exact-tree evidence.

Syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed. ### Reject unusable backup mappings before recovery (2026-10-28, source `b1d9d2f`)

Backup verification now checks that each vault has a unique recovery folder, every file maps to that vault's folder, and restore targets are unique. Malformed or colliding mappings fail verification, so Restore history does not offer a hash-valid copy that would merge vaults or fail partway through recovery; retention also leaves such a copy untouched. A synthetic regression reproduces two distinct vaults mapped to one folder and verifies the copy is excluded from recovery history. On Windows with Node.js 24.22.1, the exact code commit passed `npm run test:all`: core 497 total (491 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/permission skips), UI 29/29. Focused `test/backup.test.mjs` passed 17/17 runnable (1 Windows symlink-permission skip); `node --check src/backup.mjs` and `git diff --check` passed. This is local automated recovery evidence, not a physical second-device restore or full disaster-recovery acceptance.

### Cross-vault source references in scoped handoffs (2026-10-28)

Structured handoffs now accept source references as `path/to/note.md` within the mailbox vault or `Vault Name::path/to/note.md` across vaults. BrainLink checks each reference through the sender's `read_note` permission before saving; private, excluded, missing, and cross-Twin sources fail with one generic error. Cross-vault references are recorded as text because a separate vault cannot be represented as a reliable Obsidian wikilink. A dedicated shared mailbox no longer prevents the procedural guide from being read by an otherwise authorized scoped agent. The fictional regression uses separately tokened Company and Personal agents and confirms private-note and cross-Twin source isolation.

Verification on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1: affected core, MCP, handoff and Twin suites passed 101 tests with 1 Linux-only skip. A full `npm test` run reported 488 passed, 1 failed, and 6 platform skips while the source-reference format was being changed. The implementation was adjusted to preserve unqualified mailbox-vault paths as well as permission-checked cross-vault references; all affected suites then passed. The full suite was not rerun after this compatibility fix, so a completely green full-suite result is not claimed. No live Claude-to-Codex model handoff or release is claimed.

This is local automated evidence, not hosted CI, live model-to-model handoff, or release acceptance.

### Exact saved project routing for AI session closeout (2026-10-28)

`session_close` may now omit its vault only when the exact project identifier matches a destination previously saved by the user. BrainLink returns the vault and preview path without writing; a non-matching project still needs an explicit vault or a user choice. Twin-scoped agents are checked against the resolved vault grant before any filing operation, and denied routes do not reveal another Twin's vault name. Folder suggestions retain their existing same-vault access checks.

### Signed update manifest verification foundation (2026-10-28)

Added `src/update-manifest.mjs`, a pure verifier for detached Ed25519 release envelopes. It checks the pinned public-key ID, signature, canonical UTF-8 JSON, schema and channel, numeric non-downgrade version, package platform/architecture, checksum syntax, bounded size, and a URL under this repository's matching GitHub release tag. Synthetic-key tests pass 5/5, including payload tampering, wrong key, downgrade, wrong channel, unsafe URL, hash and size, duplicate asset, and non-canonical JSON rejection. Package smoke coverage now checks that the verifier is present and importable in staged app files; those hosted package jobs have not run for this commit. No private release key was created or stored. The verifier is not connected to Setup, has no configured trust key or manifest endpoint, does not download or hash an artifact, and does not launch an installer. Do not treat this as an updater or a signed release.

### Isolated MCP launch from the generated Codex connection (2026-10-28)

Added an end-to-end local regression test that connects Codex to a fictional BrainLink config, reads the exact command, arguments, and environment written to Codex's TOML, launches that MCP server entry, and checks that `brainlink_guide` and `vault_search` use the fictional vault. A planted private-note canary stays out of the guide and search response, while the public fixture is retrievable. The first full suite on this working tree exposed a packaging smoke-test fixture that omitted the helper imported by the connector. The fix isolates that helper in `src/app-home.mjs` and stages it with the connector. This validates BrainLink's generated Codex connection and isolated server launch without invoking a paid AI model; it does not prove the Codex application itself performed a live model handoff.

### Collision-safe handoff preview and confirmation (2026-10-28)

`send_handoff` now returns a distinct numbered path when the same sender, recipient, subject and minute already exist. The caller can pass that exact `preview_path` on confirmation, even if the clock has moved on; the path is checked against the configured mailbox and matching sender, recipient and subject. If another writer claims it after preview, BrainLink returns a conflict and never overwrites the file. The portable cross-AI handoff Skill was updated to carry the reviewed path. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/cross-client-handoff.test.mjs test/brainlink.test.mjs` passed: 80 total, 79 passed, 0 failed, 1 Linux-only skip. The focused collision and two-client tests also passed 2/2 after the final path-boundary check. This is local MCP/API evidence; real Claude and Codex model pickup remains a separate client-acceptance step.

This is local automated evidence; hosted Actions, live Claude-to-Codex continuation, live user GitHub access, second-device recovery, installed-app acceptance, and release readiness remain unverified.

### Connection status now distinguishes BrainLink checks from AI-client verification (2026-10-28)

Setup previously called its in-process MCP round trip a “live” client check. That check runs BrainLink with the client token and selected profile permissions; it does not launch the external AI app or prove that the app reloaded its MCP configuration. API receipts now label the check `brainlink_local_mcp` and explicitly return `external_client_verified: false`. English, Traditional Chinese and Portuguese now say “Configured”/“BrainLink reached,” explain the local permission check, and tell the user to restart and test from inside the AI app. Focused checks passed: `/api/client-health`, `/api/connect`, the custom-agent key check, and connection wording across the three locale files (5 targeted tests); `node --check` for the changed JavaScript, locale JSON parsing, and `git diff --check` also passed. This corrects status reporting and UX; it does not add external-client verification or replace live cross-client acceptance.

Follow-up RSI check: trend classification now treats a rise or fall in stale active-note counts as a structural regression or improvement, rather than displaying that count without reflecting it in the status. `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/rsi-observations.test.mjs test/rsi-observation-schedule.test.mjs test/rsi-ledger.test.mjs` passed 18/18, and `git diff --check` passed. Rebuilt Windows installer with Inno Setup 6.7.3; the staged package includes the corrected RSI logic. Installer SHA-256: `2A04C4DD3942B11A505DCBEB862F707A6CBB3199755FE663B98A6C8DB7BBE155`. This follow-up has focused tests, not a second full-suite run.

### Profile-scoped private session logging (2026-10-28)

Added a separate opt-in `log_session` permission for profile-scoped AIs. Setup only offers it when episodic memory is enabled and requires the action to cover every vault granted to the AI because the session tool is profile-wide. BrainLink forces these entries to be private, stamps the owning Twin profile, and checks that owner before appending to an existing session; it never writes the logged text into the granted vault. The permission appears in the Twin editor and built-in client connection flow in English, Traditional Chinese and Portuguese. Synthetic tests verify denial without the grant, forced-private storage even when the caller requests public storage, refusal to append to another profile's session, Setup acceptance/rejection, and cross-vault grant consistency. Focused session/Twin tests passed 34/34. Full local `npm run test:all` on code commit `abc05cd` passed core 467 total (461 passed, 0 failed, 6 Windows privilege/platform skips) and UI 29/29. After adding a test-only DeepSeek Harness flow, the Setup creation test and the synthetic Harness Setup-to-MCP private-session test passed; that Harness test used a local fixture and did not run inside the Harness app. `git diff --check` passed. Real cross-AI session capture remains unverified.

### Guided review of an imported session (2026-10-27)

Added an MCP `review-session` prompt that guides an authorized AI through selecting one imported session, reading only what BrainLink's session-sharing settings allow, preparing a source-aware summary, and previewing a separate private episode before asking to save. It is hidden from scoped agents unless episodic memory, session reading, and closeout/folder permissions are all available. A denied full-text read or ambiguous session requires the AI to stop and ask; the prompt cannot change access settings, alter the imported record, or save without approval. The exact imported session ID is included in the proposed episode as readable provenance and a `source_session_id` field. On Windows/Node.js 24.22.1, `test/session-close.test.mjs`, `test/session-filing.test.mjs`, `test/brainlink.test.mjs`, and `test/twin-profiles.test.mjs` passed 113 tests, 0 failed, 1 platform-only skip. `node --check` and `git diff --check` passed. This is local prompt and permission behavior, not live AI-client use or automatic transcript summarization. The full suite now also passes on commit `e4e10e5b2c1f51285d15bb27efd3ab39bd56df86`; this still does not verify live AI-client use or automatic transcript summarization.

### Twin memory-type filtering across retrieval

Twin memory switches now filter both mixed `agent_connect` context and the shared note index. The index omits notes BrainLink can identify as procedural, working, or episodic before reading their bodies; direct note reads apply the same policy. Known sources include configured role/read-first notes, tails, handoff folders, closeout targets, explicit memory-type metadata, and known episode/handoff frontmatter. Unmarked general notes remain semantic. A second policy check after indexing catches files changed during indexing, and direct reads now repeat the type check against the body they return. Synthetic canaries verify disabled-type content is absent from semantic search, direct reads, recent notes, open loops, briefs, link health, and related-note suggestions while enabled types remain available. The stale-index race is tested by changing frontmatter while preserving file size and timestamp. On Windows, `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/twin-profiles.test.mjs test/brainlink.test.mjs` passed 95 tests, 0 failures, 1 platform-only skip. Full `npm run test:all` on commit `f8c28f471d1c6dd779551eb7f7ba48b3fe91d7d0` passed core 452 total (446 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. This is local MCP evidence, not live cross-client acceptance.

Additional surface regression coverage: disabled memory canaries are absent from `vault_recent`, `open_loops`, `brain_brief`, `vault_health`, and `vault_related`, as well as search/read; other enabled memory types remain available. The focused core/Twin test receipt above includes this coverage and a stale-index read race regression.

### Session closeout destination suggestions

When an AI closes a session into an explicitly selected vault without a folder, BrainLink now reuses a saved exact-project folder or suggests one from strong note matches in that same vault. Content-based suggestions run only if the active Twin can search semantic memory; otherwise BrainLink uses the configured intake folder. The preview reports its path and suggestion reason, and the close prompt tells the AI to pass that reviewed folder when confirming. Suggestions require an enabled writable vault; the feature does not infer a destination vault or write before confirmation. Fictional-vault validation: `test/session-close.test.mjs` plus `test/session-filing.test.mjs` passed 17/17; the broader retrieval/Twin/core set passed 112 tests, 0 failures, 1 platform-only skip before the final writable-destination guard, and the two filing suites passed 17/17 again after that guard. Live AI-client closeout remains unverified.

Earlier focused work on agent connection context:

`agent_connect` can return a mixed context packet, so it now checks each content group against that Twin's memory policy before reading it: working tails, semantic recent notes/open loops/memories, and episodic capture summaries. Role notes remain procedural and the tool itself is unavailable when procedural memory is disabled. The tool description and activation prompt now tell the model to report only context it actually received. Synthetic canaries turn off each non-procedural type in turn and verify the hidden content is absent while enabled types remain available. Focused Windows tests on source commit `79430b6` passed 76 tests with 0 failures and 1 platform-only skip; clean-commit full-suite results for the policy implementation are recorded above. This verifies the local MCP boundary, not live behavior in every external AI client.

### Scheduled local RSI observation (2026-10-27)

Local aggregate-only RSI observation now has an off-by-default daily, weekly, fortnightly, or monthly schedule, plus a manual run button. Enabling the schedule triggers its first due report promptly; later checks run while the Setup background process is available. Only aggregate reports and run status are persisted; raw activity entries, note content, vault paths, and agent labels are not. Reports can suggest human-reviewed proposals but never create proposals or alter live behavior. Full Windows `npm run test:all`: core 447 total (441 passed, 0 failed, 6 host/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. Focused schedule and RSI API tests passed 12/12; `git diff --check`, JavaScript syntax checks, and locale JSON parsing passed. This verifies local scheduling, authenticated settings/manual APIs, and synthetic canary exclusion. It does not prove recursive improvement, actual customer token savings, cross-client behavior, or hosted CI.

### RSI approval receipts bound to tested source

Experiment receipts now include the Git commit and clean-tree state from before and after the run. Runs from a dirty, missing, or changing source tree are marked unbound and cannot support promotion. Acceptance also requires the exact run ID, candidate, fixture hash, and promotion commit to match an eligible receipt. Legacy schema-1 ledger records remain readable but cannot be used as new approval evidence. Focused Windows tests passed 15/15; the latest full-suite receipt is recorded at the top of this file. This binds evidence to code; it does not itself approve or deploy an RSI change.

### Opt-in per-vault RSI link-health comparison (2026-10-27)

Added an off-by-default option to compare structural link-health counts for one vault explicitly selected in the local Setup panel. The observer can report count changes in notes, links, orphans, broken links, duplicate titles, and stale active notes. It reads the selected vault locally but stores only the vault label and aggregate counts; it does not persist note bodies, paths, titles, or link targets. Changing vault scope resets the comparison baseline. This is a signal for human review, not proof that content is filed correctly, that notes are useful, or that memory quality improved. Synthetic privacy tests and focused schedule/API/UI tests passed 20/20 on the current working tree; the full suite and installer build are pending.

### Existing-folder picker for source scans

Setup now lets a user choose a scan folder from existing directories in the selected public vault instead of typing a path. The authenticated list excludes the vault root, private vaults, hidden/protected/excluded folders, and symlink traversal by reusing BrainLink's bounded safe-folder listing. Vault switching refreshes the options and retains an existing configured choice only when it remains available. ### Install DeepSeek Harness Skills through named profiles

BrainLink Skills can now be installed when the user connected DeepSeek Harness through named profiles only. The Skills remain shared in Harness's user-level `skills` folder, while MCP tokens and vault access stay isolated per profile. Setup now accepts an active `deepseek-harness@<profile>` connection as the connection gate for installing the generic Harness Skills; it does not create or widen any profile grant. The setup integration test uses a fictional named profile, verifies the separate profile connection and shared Skills install/remove flow, and still rejects installs for disconnected clients. Full Windows validation on source commit `51e0acb`: core 444 tests (438 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. Live Harness Skills discovery and tool access remain unverified.

### Install portable Agent Skills into connected AI clients

Setup shows the three versioned BrainLink skills for Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, Google Antigravity App/IDE, Antigravity CLI, Hermes Agent, and DeepSeek Harness. After connecting the client, the user can install or update the skills in that surface's user-level discovery folder, or remove BrainLink-managed copies. BrainLink records hashes so it only updates/removes unchanged files it installed; collisions, local edits, unsafe paths and damaged receipts fail closed. A pre-existing identical skill is reported as current and is not claimed. The two Antigravity surfaces use their separate documented global skill folders while sharing the connected Antigravity MCP configuration. DeepSeek Harness profiles share the configured user skill folder. Live discovery and AI use still need validation on the user's installed clients.

### Protect credentials in local AI client configs

Every local AI client config BrainLink writes now gets restrictive permissions because it contains a BrainLink agent token. On Windows, the config and token-bearing backups are ACL-checked to allow only the current user, SYSTEM, and Administrators; POSIX files use mode `0600`. This includes JSON, YAML, TOML, and Claude Code's CLI-managed config path. Also fixed Hermes setup for a valid config that has no `mcp_servers` section. On this Windows machine, `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/wiring.test.mjs` passed 30/30, including the config and backup ACL checks; `git diff --check` and `node --check src/connectors.mjs` passed. Unix permission behavior still needs a Unix-host run, and live client pickup remains unverified.

Follow-up security check: the Windows ACL test now rejects any extra partial-access grant as well as extra full-control grants, and the error for a missing user SID names BrainLink's agent token instead of one specific AI client. The focused Windows connector checks passed 2/2; syntax and diff checks passed. The full-suite receipt above predates this test-only assertion and generic error wording change.

### Reliable desktop Setup re-open

The desktop launcher now saves the active Setup port in a small per-user runtime marker. Opening the desktop icon again checks that recorded port and reuses the existing Setup page, even if BrainLink had to choose a fallback port. The marker contains only port, process ID and app version; it is removed when that Setup server closes, and stale markers are cleared on the next launch. `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-force-exit test/setup-instance.test.mjs` passed 1/1 on Windows. This covers local launcher reuse, not an installed-package shortcut or cross-user behavior.

### DeepSeek Harness MCP connector

Setup can now add, detect, and remove BrainLink's local stdio MCP entry in DeepSeek Harness's documented `cordis.patch.yml` format. It backs up the existing file, preserves unrelated YAML entries/comments, supports `DSH_HOME`, and refuses malformed YAML. The Home-level config is shared across Harness profiles on that computer, so it must use a BrainLink AI-agent token scoped to the intended Twin; it does not create a separate Harness permission system. Official reference: [DeepSeek Harness MCP configuration](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/mcp/mcp-client/README.md). On Windows, the wiring tests passed 27/27, including backup, update, disconnect, token readback, unrelated-plugin preservation, and malformed-file refusal. Live Harness installation, profile pickup/reload, tool call, and access-scope behavior are still unverified.

### Optional scheduled research-source freshness checks

Research discovery can now optionally recheck up to three oldest citations from prior BrainLink public-source discoveries after each manual or scheduled run, but only when the new setting is enabled and the source remains selected. A citation is eligible after 30 days without a check; the exact saved public arXiv ID, DOI, or GitHub repository is queried. The batch writes only fingerprint/status receipts, never edits the proposal, and reports checked/deferred counts with the run. The option is off by default; individual manual checks remain available. Privacy text is translated in all shipped locales. On source commit `0a0ded904abd8246207cc08bac00219cc14e39ed`, `npm run test:all` passed on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1: core 428 tests (422 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. This improves citation maintenance only; it does not validate source claims or make RSI autonomous.

### Chinese support for RSI research relevance

Discovery relevance scoring now tokenizes unspaced Han text into overlapping two-character terms and recognizes explicit Traditional and Simplified Chinese workflow phrases across working, semantic, procedural, and episodic memory. Signals and source metadata still need matching wording; there is no translation or Traditional/Simplified mapping, and this lexical matcher is not semantic relevance. On exact commit `25571f3a3004cd0751b9186be8232483adee17c2`, the complete `npm run test:all` passed: core 426 tests (420 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 29/29. This supports Chinese-language research metadata when the source and user's signal use matching terms, but does not establish broader retrieval or discovery quality.

### Current Windows package build and isolated runtime smoke check

After source commit `b2d91ce`, the Windows Inno Setup build completed successfully with Inno Setup 6.7.3 and produced `dist/BrainLink-Setup-1.2.8.exe` (SHA-256 `2DB22CB65DF1689C6976E85EF2E20343DFBE5A1CEA50FEAABBED6792F85A8CDC`). The staged bundled Node.js 24.22.1 runtime reported BrainLink 1.2.8 and initialized a fictional vault with `--no-scaffold` using an isolated temporary `BRAINLINK_HOME`; the temporary config showed the expected vault and default agent. The packaged Setup process then started on loopback port 51988; `/health` identified BrainLink, and the Setup page returned HTTP 200 with title `BrainLink Setup`. The process was stopped after the check. No installer was run against the user's Windows account, so PATH/Start-menu installation and uninstallation were not repeated. This is a successful local build/runtime/UI-start check, not a signed or hosted release.

Added an authenticated RSI export endpoint and a localized Setup button that downloads a dated JSON review record containing proposal data, source provenance, experiment results, status history, monitoring and rollback receipts. It exports the projected ledger records, not raw hash-chain events, adds a SHA-256 checksum for accidental-change detection, and does not mutate the ledger. Focused RSI discovery and authenticated Setup API tests pass 19/19, including checksum verification; UI tests pass 29/29; locale JSON and `git diff --check` pass. Full Windows core suite: 414 tests (407 passed, 1 failed, 6 platform skips). The failure was an unrelated scheduled-backup API test whose local HTTP connection was refused under the full run; rerunning that test alone passed 1/1. Browser download interaction remains unverified. The checksum does not authenticate who created the export.

Rechecked handoff and Twin access on commit `5982f26`: `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test test/cross-client-handoff.test.mjs test/skills.test.mjs test/twin-profiles.test.mjs` passed 21/21. This proves the fictional local MCP transfer, filtering, portable skill files and profile grants. Codex CLI `0.164.0-alpha.11.1` is installed; `claude auth status` reports `loggedIn: false`, so a real Claude model handoff cannot be run in this environment. Cross-client live acceptance remains unverified.

Backup status now tells the user the local time and whether the last attempt was manual, scheduled or during setup; old records with missing timestamps or trigger metadata get a translated fallback. The backup suite passed 16/16 runnable tests (1 Windows symlink-privilege skip), including localized receipt checks and a real fictional scheduled backup/restore API flow.

## Current working-tree update (2026-10-25)

Google Antigravity one-click MCP setup was added at its documented global path (`~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`). Focused connector tests passed 16/16, including config merge, backup, disconnect preservation, and avoiding false detection from Gemini folders. Locale tests passed 2/2. The current Windows core suite passed 325/325 runnable tests (six platform-dependent checks skipped); the separate UI suite passed 28/28. No installed Antigravity app was available for live pickup verification.

The 3D graph note panel now supports direct editing in writable vaults. The save route checks the enabled vault, direct-write setting, read-only mode, indexed path, privacy/exclusion rules, and generated-note status before writing. It preserves frontmatter, uses a SHA-256 revision check to refuse stale saves, and writes through a temporary file before rename. The panel keeps the Obsidian open action. A searchable, keyboard-accessible note list now gives an alternative to choosing notes on the 3D canvas; it shows at most 50 results and supports English, Traditional Chinese, and Portuguese. Focused graph tests pass 12/12, including privacy, save, and accessible-list checks. A headless Chrome acceptance check with fictional data selected a note from the keyboard list, opened Edit, saved a change, confirmed persistence, and restored the sample file byte-for-byte. Graph rendering is also visible in the browser. Direct mouse-node selection and real screen-reader use remain unverified. A narrow race remains possible if an external editor changes the file after the revision check but before rename.

Backup recovery was manually exercised with a fictional vault: BrainLink created a backup, verified its hashes, restored both files into a separate empty directory, preserved the private test note, and left the source note unchanged. The temporary test files were removed after the check. This confirms the local restore path only; it does not verify a user's configured scheduled destination or recovery on another computer.

The prior branch added a separately confirmed remote-conflict-copy action. It re-fetches and privacy-filters remote notes, accepts only paths still classified as conflicts, and saves remote content to a deterministic `BrainLink Conflicts/<path>.remote-<sha256-prefix>.md` copy. The local version is never overwritten. Parent directory creation rejects symlinks and non-directories; identical retries are idempotent. English, Portuguese, and Traditional Chinese UI strings are included. `docs/CHATGPT-CODE-CONTRIBUTOR-PROMPT.md` defines a non-overlapping held-out retrieval/token-efficiency evaluation for a separate Codex contributor.

Previous branch validation on Windows with Node 24.18.0: `npm run test:all` completed successfully: core 294 tests (288 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped), UI 28 tests (28 passed, 0 failed). Focused GitHub and locale tests passed 15/15, including the authenticated Setup endpoint. Current branch validation is recorded below after the profile-scoped repository changes. These tests use fictional vaults and local bare Git remotes. The demo Setup page was visually opened, but browser automation stopped before the GitHub section could be inspected; no live GitHub account or remote repository was used. The change is not cross-client validated or release-ready.

Current integration combines Claude's confirmed profile assignment model, the remote conflict-copy action, opt-in RSI research discovery, and the third contributor's independent retrieval evaluator. Each Twin profile binds one canonical GitHub repository identity, clone path, non-private vault, either a folder allowlist or an explicit whole-vault confirmation, and a confirmation timestamp. Setup review and explicit confirmation create the assignment; GitHub operations validate profile, vault, assignment, and current clone origin before accessing note candidates. Profile commits carry a profile trailer checked before push. Conflict copies use the same profile authorization gate and preserve the local version. All GitHub operations now fail closed until a Twin profile and repository assignment establish the data owner; legacy configs still load without alteration. RSI discovery queries only the fixed arXiv feed, creates sourced low-confidence proposals, and never accepts or implements them. It is off by default, and enabled/unpaused discovery keeps the app available for its scheduled checks. Private backups include discovery settings and receipts alongside the RSI ledger. The independent retrieval fixture and evaluator are implemented, but its candidate compaction policy is rejected by its own quality gate and does not change production ranking. Automated tests use fictional profiles/vaults, synthetic feed responses, and an order-invariance check for source recall. Live GitHub account permissions, live research-feed access, desktop UX on Windows, cross-device use, and cross-client behavior remain unverified.
