### Raw-to-Wiki reference review (2026-10-25)

Reviewed Ian's supplied 167-second reel transcript and selected stills. BrainLink has a manual scan and an opt-in daily scan at a user-selected local time for up to 20 non-private vault folders. Setup displays last-run status and the manual queue. The schedule works while BrainLink is running; the existing launch-at-sign-in option can start it after login. Scans detect eligible new/changed Markdown, TXT, CSV/TSV, JSON, XML, YAML and LOG files and prepare a review queue; they do not summarize with an AI or file knowledge unattended. The portable `brainlink-raw-to-wiki` skill teaches compatible clients to read selected sources by queue ID/revision, search for duplicates, preview a linked note, wait for user approval, and record a content-free outcome. PDFs, Office files, images, HTML and continuous file-system watching remain unimplemented. Focused tests cover privacy, schedule and manual scan APIs, the skill manifest and approval order, and profile grants. See `docs/RAW-WIKI-INGESTION-MODEL.md`.

Windows validation: Node.js 24.18.0; serialized core run `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 "test/*.test.mjs"` passed 337 tests (331 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips); `npm run test:ui` passed 28/28. A focused schedule, backup, and Twin-profile run passed 20 tests with one Windows symlink skip. A parallel full-suite attempt showed timing failures in unrelated tests and stalled; it was stopped and replaced by the passing serialized run. Tests use fictional vaults. Native login-at-start acceptance and behavior after the user closes BrainLink remain unverified; token savings are not measured.

On 2026-10-26, added the portable `brainlink-raw-to-wiki` Agent Skill in `skills/` with byte-identical Claude Code and Codex copies. Its instructions require explicit folder scope, same-vault source links, duplicate search, preview and approval before writing, and a receipt only after BrainLink confirms the note exists. Skill, schedule, and scan UI tests passed 5/5; no live Claude Code, Codex, or other client execution was performed.

### Scheduled source-scan retry (2026-10-26)

Scheduled Raw-to-Wiki scans now retry a failed run twice, 15 minutes apart, for at most three attempts per scheduled day. The retry state is stored in local configuration; scan status remains content-free. Saving unchanged settings preserves a pending retry, while changing the schedule clears stale retry state. Turning the schedule off pauses retries; manual Scan now remains available. `test/ingestion-schedule.test.mjs` passes 4/4, including recovery when a missing source folder returns. The full core suite passed 388 tests (382 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) before this retry change; full-suite coverage of the new change is pending. No unattended AI processing or auto-filing was added.

### Multi-folder source scanning (2026-10-27)

Setup now lets users manage up to 20 scan folders across public vaults. A manual run scans the selected list and keeps each result tied to its own vault; one daily schedule scans the same targets. Private or unknown vaults, hidden paths, duplicate targets, and unsafe relative paths are rejected. Existing one-folder configurations load as a one-target list. Marking a target vault private or removing it removes those targets from the schedule and clears stale retry state. `test/ingestion-schedule.test.mjs` passes 4/4, including two-vault scheduled and manual scans, legacy migration, and private/unknown-vault rejection. UI locale and inline-script syntax checks pass. The full core suite has not been rerun after this change; no live desktop or customer-vault validation is claimed.

Full Windows validation for this implementation: Node.js 24.18.0, `npm run test:all`, core 334 tests (328 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28 tests (28 passed, 0 failed). The skips are platform or Windows symlink-privilege dependent. `git diff --check` passed. This validates the local code path and fictional test data only; it does not verify a live user vault, background monitoring, token savings, cross-client behavior, or release readiness.

### End-of-session review link (2026-10-25)

SessionEnd reminders now link to an already imported Claude Code or Codex session when the cue's client and exact session ID match one unique transcript source. The API returns only an opaque BrainLink session reference; it does not expose the client session ID, transcript path, working directory, summary, or messages. Matching reads session metadata only. The Sessions UI's explicit **Review session** action opens that private summary for the selected record and offers the existing preview-and-confirm **File summary** flow. Missing or ambiguous matches do not produce a link. This joins the end cue to the existing session-filing path without enabling automatic transcript capture or unapproved note writes. Windows focused validation across end-cue, closeout, filing, and RSI discovery passed 31/31; the fictional Codex API case verifies summary withholding until the explicit review request. Full `npm run test:all`: core 320 tests (314 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28/28. The six core skips are platform or Windows symlink-privilege dependent. Also fixed a reproduced intermittent Windows `EPERM` during RSI discovery settings replacement with bounded retry and temp-file cleanup. Live Claude Code/Codex hook lifecycle and browser acceptance remain unverified.

Validation on Windows with Node 24.18.0: `npm run test:all` passed: core 318 tests (312 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28 tests (28 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped), 346 total (340 passed, 6 skipped). The six skipped core tests require Linux or macOS, or Windows symlink privileges. Retrieval evaluator tests passed 7/7; the RSI discovery and backup focus run passed 22 tests (21 passed, one Windows symlink case skipped). ### Independent retrieval evaluation contribution (2026-10-25)

Created feature branch `eval/independent-retrieval-efficiency` from freshly fetched `fix/private-session-excerpts` HEAD `807619a`. Added a frozen, fictional 30-case set (fixture size 6,217 bytes; SHA-256 `500e0faffedcdf194c535dc3c0b2f44afe2111814f0d6c034420616392cb5490`) and a schema-2 mode in the existing read-only evaluator. On macOS with Node `v26.3.1`, baseline task success was 17/30, top-k all-source correctness was 22/22 answerable cases, source recall 1.000, mean estimated context 497 tokens, and 0 privacy violations. The fixed label-blind compaction candidate scored 15/30, 18/22 all-source cases, source recall 0.800, mean estimated context 219 tokens, and 0 privacy violations. The evaluator returned `quality_regression`; the 56% lower token estimate is not counted as an improvement because required source/context quality regressed. The task score is a source/context and abstention proxy, not generated-answer quality or provider billing. Machine-readable receipt: `samples/evaluation/reports/retrieval-independent-v1.json`, bound to evaluator source commit `ec6bbfd`.

Focused evaluator tests passed 7/7; the standalone UI suite passed 28/28. `npm run test:all` was attempted twice and consistently stopped in the core suite at 291 passed, 4 failed, 1 skipped out of 296; the four failures are in existing GitHub conflict-copy/local-commit tests and a Twin session-assignment test. Running those specs at the unchanged baseline commit `807619a` reproduced the same 4 failures (17 passed, 4 failed), confirming they predate this contribution. The full UI suite was run separately and passed. No production behavior, client data, network lookup, provider call, or remote evaluation was used.

Named-agent runtime now enforces each memory type across tool discovery/calls, prompts, and resources, in addition to vault and operation grants. Session reads enforce stored `profile_id` before query matching or returning metadata/excerpts/messages. Assignment is one-way and always private; the session cannot be switched to shared. Private closeouts can be enabled only by `propose_note` plus filing-folder grants. Handoff read/send each require separate grants; records carry a profile owner and are filtered before mailbox bodies are read. Replies are append-only, same-profile-linked updates with validated lifecycle status; the local receipt shows the latest status and update subject. | `src/twin-profiles.mjs`, `src/sessions/store.mjs`, `src/server.mjs`, `src/setup.mjs`, `src/writes.mjs`, `vendor/brainlink-store`, `docs/TWIN-PROFILES.md`, `docs/HANDOFF-LIFECYCLE.md`, `test/twin-profiles.test.mjs`. Fictional tests check each disabled memory type against advertised tools and direct calls; procedural resources and episodic/semantic prompts are also checked. Existing Personal, Organisation, Client-profile, and unassigned-session canaries remain. Assignment, same-profile acknowledgement, foreign-profile acknowledgement, and cross-profile handoff canaries pass locally. General note writes and session logging remain unavailable. Platform clients without named-agent profile tokens still use their existing scopes; full Personal/Organisation/Client separation across clients and data surfaces is not yet proven. |

### Live MCP client checks (2026-10-27)

Hermes Agent 0.21.0's `hermes mcp test brainlink` started the installed BrainLink stdio server and reported a successful connection with 22 tools discovered (27.3 seconds). Claude Code 2.1.310's MCP health check also connected to this checkout's server using a temporary isolated profile; no model was invoked and the normal Claude configuration was not changed. Codex CLI 0.164.0-alpha.11.1 accepted an isolated temporary MCP config, but `codex mcp list` only confirmed that the entry was enabled; it did not test server startup. The normal Codex config had no BrainLink entry. These checks prove client-to-server startup/tool discovery for Hermes and Claude in the stated environments, not Claude-to-Codex task handoff, named-Twin privacy across real clients, Harness pickup, or customer acceptance. The temporary profiles use only temporary data homes and are not product configuration. No source code changed during these checks.

### GitHub owner boundary (2026-10-25)

The legacy behavior that permitted GitHub sharing without any Twin profile has been removed. A setup with no profiles now fails closed for candidate listing, preview, commit, push, remote import and conflict-copy operations; the local UI explains that a Personal, Organisation or Client Twin and a confirmed repository assignment are required. Existing configs and vault data still load unchanged; the user must explicitly create the owner profile and confirm the repository before those operations resume. Focused GitHub tests passed 34/34. Full Windows `npm run test:all` passed: core 320 tests (314 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28/28. The six skips are platform or symlink-privilege dependent. This change uses fictional data and does not contact live GitHub.

### RSI research-source expansion (2026-10-25)

Opt-in research discovery now supports user-selected arXiv and Crossref metadata endpoints. The request still sends only configured topic terms; problem statements, vault/session content, and credentials remain local. Both adapters use fixed HTTPS host/path allowlists, DNS checks, redirect refusal, bounded responses, source-aware parsing, hostile-content filtering, and provenance-linked `discovered` proposals. Crossref stores only a short abstract excerpt and DOI citation; its documentation warns that some abstracts may be copyrighted. Focused discovery and RSI ledger tests passed 24/24; full Windows `npm run test:all` passed: core 322 tests (316 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28/28. These are synthetic-response and local UI tests; no live Crossref/arXiv response, automatic experiment, promotion, or recursive gain was validated.

### RSI source-freshness receipts (2026-10-25)

Setup now offers a manual source check for discovered arXiv/Crossref proposals. It derives a fixed-endpoint lookup from the stored paper ID or DOI, and appends only a status, timestamp, and SHA-256 metadata fingerprints to the discovery receipt file; it never updates the proposal or stores a second abstract/title snapshot. The UI distinguishes changed, unchanged, missing, unsafe, unavailable, and paused results. Focused RSI discovery + ledger validation passed 25/25. Full Windows `npm run test:all` passed: core 323 tests (317 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28/28. Tests used synthetic responses; live source behavior remains unverified, and freshness status does not verify claim truth or automatically revise citations.

### Bounded RSI experiment receipts (2026-10-25)

Setup can run either of two built-in fictional retrieval suites for an RSI idea in `testing`. The proposal's candidate version must match the suite. The runner uses an isolated app-data folder and fixed fixture paths; it runs no proposal-supplied code and returns aggregate metrics, privacy counts, fixture hash, and limitations without query text, paths, snippets, or per-case results. Receipts append to the hash-linked ledger and do not change status or live retrieval. The UI now shows plain-language outcomes, quality rates and estimated token counts in English, Traditional Chinese and Portuguese. Focused RSI tests passed 10/10. Full Windows `npm run test:all` passed: core 329 tests (323 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28/28. This validates the local code path and fictional fixtures only; it does not prove independent evaluation, real token savings, cross-client behavior, production readiness, or recursive gain.

### Sender-reported Git checkpoint in handoffs (2026-10-25)

`send_handoff` accepts an optional, checked Git checkpoint and shows it in the preview and saved handoff. The mailbox reader returns it as `reported`; the sender's checkout is not visible to BrainLink. The shared handoff Skill instructs the receiving agent to inspect its own repo, branch and dirty state, and never switch branches automatically. Local fictional tests reject URLs, paths, unsafe branch names, invalid SHAs, and out-of-range ahead counts. Live client pickup remains unverified.

### Receipt-gated GitHub note updates (2026-10-26)

Remote pull can update a note BrainLink previously imported only when its local bytes still match a saved SHA-256 receipt and fetched Git history descends from the imported commit. Updates require per-note selection and confirmation; conflict copies and add-only behavior remain. The receipt stores identifiers, commit and content hash, not note text. Updates use a temporary sibling file, recheck the local bytes, then atomically replace the note. Focused local bare-remote tests verify first import, body-free receipts, fast-forward update, preservation of a later local edit, and refusing a rewritten history. Full Windows `npm run test:all` passed: core 349 tests (343 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/symlink skips) and UI 28/28; after adding the rewritten-history assertion, the focused update test passed. GitHub.com account access, desktop UI use, another device and another AI client remain untested; losing the local receipt safely makes a changed note a conflict.

After adding direct regression checks for the GitHub pull UI, Windows `npm run test:all` passed on commit `f3bc0fed7b0ac40ee8d9e430bb5c9eddb5752567`: core 351 tests (345 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. The new checks confirm updates are opt-in, conflict copies stay a separate action, and the confirmation clearly describes the local copy in English, Portuguese and Traditional Chinese. `git diff --check` passed. This remains local UI and local bare-remote evidence; it does not verify live GitHub access or desktop acceptance.

## Remaining work, in order of evidence needed

1. **Real cross-AI use:** sign in to Claude Code, then verify one Claude-to-Codex handoff through pickup, reply, completion, and private-note isolation. The local fictional protocol test and Codex-only live probe do not prove this flow.
2. **Live GitHub use:** run profile assignment, preview, exact commit/push, import, conflict handling, and opt-in sync in Setup against a user-controlled test repository. Current tests use a local bare remote. The latest Actions attempt for `2dcf744` failed before any job step ran; obtain real Node 22/24 and package-build results.
3. **Session closeout:** exercise Claude Code and Codex lifecycle hooks in normal sessions; verify the local-model draft with an installed model; test destination recommendations and correction on real but approved workflows; investigate an optional Harness `session.end` integration only after a version-specific isolated permission review. Keep raw transcript reading opt-in and note filing approval-gated.
4. **Efficiency proof:** measure actual provider token use and user time on a new held-out project set, while checking answer quality and source recall. Existing evaluator results use fictional data and estimated payload size; lower estimates alone are not measured user savings.
5. **RSI loop:** test proposals on fresh independent sets and connect post-release checks to trusted measurements. A reviewed rollback receipt is implemented in the append-only ledger; it records a rollback only after a person has reverted and checked the code, and does not run Git or change live behavior. The current observer and proposal flow still need a human and do not prove repeated self-improvement.
6. **Desktop and recovery acceptance:** test sign-in launch in real per-user desktop sessions, restore from an external backup destination on another device, accessibility, large-vault speed, upgrades, and common user tasks. Existing package checks cover isolated environments, not customer acceptance.
7. **Release readiness:** complete signing, update and rollback delivery, support and commercial terms, privacy/security review, and cross-platform release evidence before describing BrainLink as ready to sell.

## Current limits

- This snapshot does not assign an overall completion percentage: the product is a set of capabilities with different evidence gates, and a percentage would imply precision not supported by the receipts.
- The current test receipt is local to the stated Windows/Node environment; six platform/privilege-dependent tests were skipped.
- The latest GitHub Actions attempt failed before any job step ran; it is not a code-test result.
- No production deployment, customer acceptance, real cross-client handoff, or commercial readiness is claimed.

### Twin profile creation in local Setup (2026-10-26)

Local Setup now creates and edits Personal, Organisation, or Client Twin profiles without hand-editing `config.json`. The form collects available non-private vaults, read/propose access, named AI agents and a limited operation list, plus the four memory switches; both create and edit require confirmation that lists the chosen vaults/access, agent actions, and memory types. The authenticated endpoints revalidate all selections and the complete resulting config before writing it. Editing preserves profile IDs and source policies, keeps GitHub assignments, and refuses removing the vault bound to GitHub. Twin type and owner are fixed after creation, so saved sessions and handoffs cannot be relabeled across privacy boundaries; the update confirmation warns that session access can include older Twin sessions. New profiles are private-by-default, require provenance, and use metadata-only session capture. Focused Twin, locale, and UI-safety tests passed 23/23. On Windows with Node.js 24.22.1, full `npm run test:all` passed: core 353 tests (347 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. The disposable fictional demo rendered the create form and reached its confirmation dialog, but browser automation timed out after accepting it, so visible end-to-end save and edit remain unverified. This does not establish full isolation for preset platform clients or create a GitHub repository assignment.

### Per-vault Twin editor permission guard (2026-10-26)

The simple Twin editor now refuses to edit profiles when any assigned agent has different access or operation grants across that profile's vaults. This prevents the single shared action set in the form from widening a narrower vault grant. The Setup status exposes whether this editor can safely edit the profile; the UI hides editing for mixed-scope profiles and explains why. A regression test confirms the authenticated update endpoint rejects the edit and leaves the config bytes unchanged. Full Windows validation on Node.js 24.22.1 completed with `npm run test:all`: core 354 tests (348 passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped) and UI 28/28 (0 failed). The six skipped tests require platform or symlink privileges unavailable in this run. This is local automated evidence; mixed-scope permission management still needs a per-vault matrix editor, and desktop visual acceptance remains open.

### Per-vault Twin permissions in Setup (2026-10-26)

Twin profile creation and editing now show a permission matrix: select an agent, select which profile vaults it can access, and choose actions separately for each vault. Confirmations list the specific per-vault grants. Server checks reject grants for unselected vaults, duplicate vault grants, empty action lists, unsupported actions, and `propose_note` when that vault's profile maximum is read-only. Existing callers that send one action list per agent keep uniform access across selected vaults. Tests create and edit fictional two-vault profiles, verify different actions per vault, and prove an invalid proposal does not alter saved config. ### Tokenless AI connections fail closed (2026-10-26)

When any Twin profile exists, MCP contexts without a registered AI-agent token now receive no vaults or tools. Unknown/revoked tokens remain unassigned. The local Setup UI, background jobs, and explicit CLI maintenance commands use a marked trusted-local context; the MCP `serve` path does not. Setup reports a clear reason when a preset connector lacks Twin permission and refuses the live connection instead of silently giving it broad access. The initial full run exposed 16 component tests that had not declared their trusted-local role; their fixtures now state that role explicitly, while the runtime denial remains the default. ### Profile-scoped preset AI connections (2026-10-26)

Setup now lets a user assign a built-in AI app to one Twin profile, select only its non-private vaults, and grant search and note-reading actions. Each app receives its own random token; BrainLink stores only the token hash, while the client keeps the token in its local MCP settings. The connection check makes an in-process MCP call using that exact scoped token and selected vault. A failed check restores the prior BrainLink config and the exact prior client config. Disconnect revokes the token before removing the client entry. Bulk connect is hidden and rejected when Twin profiles exist, so it cannot bypass these choices. The flow is available in English, Traditional Chinese, and Portuguese. Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed before the final isolated Codex TOML-reader test: core 356 tests (350 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 28/28; the added TOML token-reader test then passed 1/1. Native AI-app restart/pickup, real Claude/Codex cross-client handoff, installed desktop usability, and end-user access isolation remain unverified; this is not a release-readiness claim.

### Preset AI session and handoff permissions (2026-10-26)

The built-in AI connection flow now supports separately selected per-vault note search/read, approved session history, private session-closeout proposals, and handoff read/send. Session history applies across the selected connection, so the server rejects mixed grants that would otherwise hide session tools or imply narrower access than the runtime can enforce. Handoff actions are offered only on the configured mailbox vault and require working memory; closeout proposals require episodic and semantic memory plus propose access. The health check chooses a safe read-only tool or lists available tools when a connection is write-only. ### Atomic local backup publication (2026-10-26)

A new backup is assembled under a unique hidden staging folder and renamed to its final visible name only after all vault files, artifacts, and the manifest are written. A handled write failure removes only that invocation's staging folder and preserves earlier verified backups. Abrupt process or power loss can still leave a `.partial` folder, which is not a valid backup. Tests verify success exposes only complete backups and a forced artifact failure cleans its partial copy without damaging an earlier backup. Full Windows validation on Node.js 24.22.1 passed `npm run test:all`: core 360 tests (354 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. `git diff --check` passed. The six core skips require OS support or symlink privileges unavailable on this host. This does not add encryption or retention.

### Verified-backup retention control (2026-10-26)

Setup now lets the user keep all copies or choose 5, 10, 30, or 90. Unlimited retention is the default. After making and hash-verifying the new copy, BrainLink removes only older direct-child folders whose complete file list exactly matches a valid BrainLink manifest and matching hashes. Damaged, partial, symbolic-link, unknown, and user-modified folders stay untouched; the newest backup is retained. Settings and status are localized in English, Traditional Chinese, and Portuguese. Focused backup/config/locale tests passed 13, 0 failed, 1 Windows symlink privilege skip. Final full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 361 tests (355 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. The six skips need OS or symlink privileges unavailable on this host. This is local automated evidence; native desktop review, interrupted-device testing, encrypted backup and cross-device restore remain open.

### Optional encrypted scheduled backups (2026-10-26)

Backup Settings can now encrypt new copies with AES-256-GCM. File names, vault names and the manifest are encrypted; stored object names are random. A random 256-bit recovery key is created on request, stored outside the vault and backup destination in per-user app data, and never put in the BrainLink config. Windows uses Local AppData; POSIX key files require owner-only permissions. Scheduled backups reuse the local key. Restore accepts a pasted key for another device, while old unencrypted backups remain compatible. The UI warns that turning encryption on does not change earlier copies and lets the user show/copy the key. Tests cover wrong keys, hidden names/content, encrypted restore, encrypted retention, key creation, permissions/path policy and the authenticated Setup flow. Final Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 366 tests (360 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28 tests (28 passed, 0 skipped). The six core skips need OS or symlink privileges unavailable on this host. This remains local automated evidence: another-device recovery and native desktop review are still open. The later Windows ACL regression test and scope are recorded in the 2026-10-28 receipt. Encryption is opt-in; malware or another process with the same user access can read the local key, and a lost recovery key cannot be restored.

### Recovery-key confirmation before first encryption (2026-10-26)

When a user first enables encrypted backups, Setup now asks them to paste the displayed recovery key again. The authenticated backend compares it to the locally stored 256-bit key using a constant-time comparison, does not save the confirmation, and refuses a mismatch before making a backup or changing the encryption setting. After encryption is already enabled, ordinary schedule edits do not ask for the key again. The confirmation field is cleared after a successful save. English, Traditional Chinese, and Portuguese labels are included. The focused backup suite passed 13 tests (0 failed, 1 Windows symlink-privilege skip). Full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 366 tests (360 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28 tests (28 passed, 0 failed). This improves the chance that users preserve the key but does not prove that they stored it outside the device; separate-device recovery and native desktop acceptance remain open.

### Verified backup history for simpler restore (2026-10-26)

Settings now has **Find verified backups**. It checks up to the 20 most recent named backup folders in the configured destination, verifies each file hash, hides manifest vault/file names from the list, and only offers valid copies. Selecting a copy fills the existing restore source field; restore still verifies again, requires a separate empty destination and explicit confirmation, and does not change live vaults. ### Refresh newly ended AI sessions without restarting (2026-10-26)

Session import used to be memoized for the lifetime of the MCP/Setup process, so a Claude Code or Codex transcript created after the first session read could remain absent until restart. Session reads now use incremental file cursors with a 15-second normal-read cooldown. Opening the Sessions list or reviewing end cues forces a fresh scan, allowing an exact same-client end cue to link to a newly ended local transcript without restarting. The cue still discards transcript paths and contents; its response contains only an opaque session reference. Focused fixtures verify that routine reads honor the cooldown, explicit refresh imports a new synthetic Codex transcript, and the imported record remains private. Live Claude/Codex runtime and hook lifecycle are still not validated.

Focused session validation passed 33/33. Full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 374 tests (368 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. The skips require OS/symlink privileges unavailable on this machine. This is local fixture evidence; live Claude/Codex hooks and cross-client pickup are still unverified.

### Human-reviewed typed graph relationship proposals (2026-10-26)

`propose_note` now accepts typed entity metadata and evidence-backed relationship candidates for the allowlisted entity and predicate types. BrainLink forces typed notes to `status: proposed` and edges to `review: proposed`, even if an AI supplies `status: current`; candidates are omitted from the graph until a person reviews and edits the note. Before saving a candidate, the target ID must resolve to one unique, current, visible typed note in the same vault and scope. Private, excluded, duplicate, unknown, cross-vault, and cross-scope targets receive a generic refusal that does not reveal their ID. The active Twin profile also constrains entity scope. The portable Raw-to-Wiki Skill and MCP guidance now explain this review boundary. Tests cover the MCP schema, proposed-to-verified transition, invalid candidates, profile mismatch, and a private-target canary. Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 377 tests (371 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. This does not yet provide automatic relationship extraction, a dedicated review UI, live cross-client verification, or measured token/answer-quality gains. Rollback: revert the implementation, skill copies, docs, and tests in this commit; no migration is needed.

### Local scheduled briefs (2026-10-26)

Setup now offers a daily brief for one explicitly selected, existing vault at a chosen local time, plus a manual Run now action. The scheduled job reuses the existing privacy-filtered `brain_brief` logic and presents changed-note titles, open tasks, next actions, pending decisions/questions, and link-health counts. It stores the result in the current user's local BrainLink app data, not in the vault, and does not send a notification. The schedule is off by default; enabling it keeps the local Setup background process alive until disabled. The user may choose a private vault explicitly in local Setup; note-level private items remain filtered, connected agents do not receive the brief, and displayed note-derived text is HTML-escaped. Synthetic tests cover schedule timing, manual and scheduled runs, private note/vault canaries, the read-only vault guarantee, and all three locales. Initial Windows Node.js 24.22.1 validation passed: core 380 tests (374 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. The skipped tests require OS/symlink privileges unavailable on this machine. Rollback: revert this feature's config, scheduler, Setup, UI, locale, docs, and test changes; no migration is required.

Daily Brief now allows an explicit choice of any configured, existing vault, including a private Personal vault. ### RSI promotion linked to Git revision (2026-10-26)

An RSI idea cannot enter `accepted` unless its record includes a full 40-character Git commit SHA for the promoted change, in addition to its baseline/candidate versions, test set, measured results, reviewer, decision, guardrails, and rollback. The append-only ledger records the SHA, and the local UI explains that format validation does not verify the commit on GitHub. Focused tests cover missing/malformed commit IDs, a successful synthetic acceptance record, and all shipped locales. This improves traceability; it does not verify that a commit exists, passed CI, or was deployed.

### Human-reviewed RSI post-release checks (2026-10-26)

Accepted RSI changes can now append a monitoring receipt tied to the exact promotion commit. The local Setup form records the check window, test-set/source name, sample count, main metric before/after, guardrail status and results, reviewer, and optional evidence reference. The ledger rejects a different commit, future or invalid dates, inconsistent stable/inconclusive guardrail choices, stale revisions, and unauthenticated API calls. The receipt is append-only, and the proposal stays `accepted`; a regression requires a person to make the separate rollback decision. This is a human-entered record, not automatic production telemetry, verified source data, or rollback. Windows Node.js 24.22.1 focused RSI and locale validation passed 11/11. The first full run exposed an intermittent Windows `EPERM` while replacing the discovery settings file; Windows replacement now retries for up to one second, and the scheduled discovery API test passed five repeated runs. The complete serialized `npm run test:all` rerun passed: core 380 tests (374 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. The six skips require Windows symlink privileges unavailable on this machine. Discovery tests passed 19/19. `git diff --check` passed. No live production monitoring source or automatic rollback was tested.

### Active desktop graph refresh after vault changes (2026-10-26)

The actual `brainlink ui` Setup server now watches the configured Markdown vaults and sends an authenticated Server-Sent Event with no file path or note content. The active 3D graph reloads when visible, preserves the selected note when it still exists, and postpones refresh while its editor is open. Added/removed vaults replace the watcher set. Recursive `fs.watch` is used where supported; otherwise BrainLink compares Markdown file path, size, and modification time every 10 seconds. A synthetic HTTP integration creates a public and private note, verifies the event contains no canary text or paths, and verifies the graph includes only the public note. Full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 381 tests (375 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. This does not test a packaged native desktop, Linux polling behavior, or large-vault polling load.

### Human-reviewed graph relationship suggestions (2026-10-26)

The graph note panel now shows valid proposed relationships with their evidence and confidence. The user can approve one connection after a confirmation prompt. The authenticated Setup route rechecks the note, candidate target, vault scope, and content revision, then updates only that relation's review state and promotes its proposed source record to current. The normal graph builder still independently validates the edge before displaying it. Tests verify that private, duplicate, cross-scope, invalid, and stale candidates cannot be approved, and that an approved candidate appears as a typed graph edge. The approval text is localized in English, Traditional Chinese, and Portuguese. Focused graph, typed-graph and locale tests passed 24/24 on Windows Node.js 24.22.1. This does not automatically extract relationships; it only makes human review of AI-proposed links usable in the app.

### Rule-based relationship discovery for graph review (2026-10-26)

Opening a typed note now scans its own text for a small allowlist of clear relation phrases and unique typed targets in the same vault and Twin scope (for example, “consumes Question data”). BrainLink displays the matching sentence as evidence and labels it as a text-pattern match, not a verified fact. The user can save it as a `review: proposed` frontmatter candidate; a separate confirmation and review action is still required before it appears as a graph edge. The route re-runs discovery, checks the current note revision, and refuses private, excluded, duplicate, cross-scope, stale, or no-longer-matching candidates. No model call or content upload is used. The heuristic abstains on source bodies over 200,000 characters or indexes with over 5,000 typed entities; its confidence field is a neutral 0.5 placeholder and is not calibrated. Focused graph, typed-graph, and locale tests passed 28/28. Full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 388 tests (382 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 28/28. This is a bounded matching aid, not automatic fact extraction or autonomous memory editing. Live cross-client handoff and packaged desktop acceptance remain unverified.

### Review and correct a just-filed session summary in active Setup (2026-10-26)

After the local Sessions screen files a summary, it offers a same-session control to correct only its `## Session summary` section or withdraw the proposal. Users can also explicitly search one selected vault for previously filed private proposals; the list returns titles and paths only, is capped at 100 results and 20,000 scanned Markdown files, and loads summary text only after the user opens a result. The authenticated local endpoint requires an existing session, an enabled writable vault, an included non-symlink Markdown path, exact `type: episode` + `private: true` + `status: proposed` metadata, a matching `BrainLink session <id>` source, and a current SHA-256 etag. Corrections reject detected secrets, preserve the rest of the note, and add a dated value-free history entry. Withdrawal changes status to `withdrawn` without deleting the session or note. Synthetic API tests cover token authentication, selected-vault isolation, exact source matching, stale-write refusal, correction, withdrawal and private-content retention. Validation: focused filing API test passed; session filing and close tests passed 11/11; host UI-model tests passed 29/29; Setup inline JavaScript syntax, all three locale JSON files and `git diff --check` passed. This feature has not had an installed-app/browser usability review. Live Claude/Codex handoff, packaged desktop acceptance and measured token/time savings remain open.

### Opt-in scheduled GitHub knowledge sync (2026-10-27)

Added per-Twin automatic GitHub sync controls to Setup. Automatic sync is off by default; enabling it requires a separate confirmation naming the Twin and repository. The user can choose 1, 6, 12, or 24 hour checks or run immediately. The scheduler keeps the local BrainLink process alive when enabled and records a content-free run receipt in local app data. Each run reuses profile/vault/repository authorization, secret and private-note filtering, safe add-only or receipt-gated remote updates, clean-checkout checks, exact selected-file commits, and no-force-push validation. Any remote conflict or selected local repository edit pauses the run. The scheduler rechecks opt-in and the complete repository assignment before each write, so disabling sync or changing scope stops the next write. A failed push retains the exact BrainLink commit/path receipt and retries only that commit; candidate batching rotates to avoid starving notes after the first 100. Disabling or clearing the assignment turns off future scheduled runs. Focused orchestration tests cover default-off refusal, import/publish sequence, conflict and dirty-worktree pauses, opt-out during a run, and pending-commit retry; Setup API tests cover opt-in confirmation and interval validation. The scheduled end-to-end test uses a temporary local bare remote and fictional private/out-of-scope canaries. Git commits and pushes use the exact paths Git stages after normalization, including on Windows. Full Windows `npm run test:all` passed on Node.js 24.22.1: core 397 total (391 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. The six skips need OS or Windows symlink privileges unavailable on this host. This does not prove a live GitHub account, a full scheduled run against a user-controlled remote, cross-device operation, or installed-app usability. Official Hermes session-storage documentation identifies `%LOCALAPPDATA%/hermes` as the Windows default, while the MCP quick start uses `~/.hermes` as its cross-platform example. ### Typed relationship rejection (2026-10-27)

The graph review queue and note panel let a user reject an unverified relationship. The source note panel lists rejected suggestions and can return one to review, preserving its evidence. ### Transactional backup restore (2026-10-27)

Restore now stages files in a temporary sibling folder, reads each recovered file back and checks its saved size and SHA-256, then publishes the complete folder with a same-volume rename. If a copy fails partway through, BrainLink removes only its temporary folder and keeps the user-selected empty destination empty. If the final publish fails, it attempts to put the original empty folder back. A fixture with a duplicated manifest entry forces a mid-restore write failure and verifies cleanup. The full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 403 tests (397 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. The six skips need OS or Windows symlink privileges unavailable on this host. Cross-device restore and failure cases on real backup drives remain unverified.

The local RSI ledger now requires a dedicated receipt to mark an accepted idea as rolled back. ### Session-close partial-save recovery response (2026-10-27)

If a confirmed close saves its private session but vault-note filing throws, the MCP response now explicitly returns `ok: false`, `session_saved: true`, `filing_status: "unknown"`, the exact session ID, and a clear recovery instruction to open Sessions and use **File summary**. If filing returns without writing, the response instead marks `filing_status: "not_written"`. Raw filesystem errors and unfiled proposal content are withheld from these recovery responses. Close requests hold a cross-process lock across duplicate check, session write, and note filing; the store reloads the latest JSONL snapshot while holding the lock before every read or write, preventing stale snapshots from replacing other sessions. A spawned two-process regression verifies that a duplicate close preserves its first outcome and that unrelated concurrent closes both persist. On Windows with Node.js 24.22.1, the current `npm run test:all` passed: core 413 tests (407 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. All six skips require unavailable OS or Windows symlink privileges. Malformed or unverifiable lock files fail closed and may need manual removal after a crash.

### Session journal corruption protection and hosted CI billing block (2026-10-27)

The session store now propagates malformed JSONL errors rather than treating a corrupt journal as an empty session list. A focused regression confirms the original journal bytes remain unchanged. The session close, store, Twin-profile and portable Skill suites passed 36/36 on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1; `git diff --check` passed. GitHub Actions run [40413582944](https://github.com/iantsai886/brainlink/actions/runs/40413582944), for commit `e903a4c`, reports that all nine jobs were not started because account payments failed or the spending limit must be increased; each job has zero steps and no runner assigned. This is an account billing blocker, not a code-test result. Resolve it in GitHub **Billing & plans** before expecting hosted test or package evidence.

### Closeout now reviews reusable memory beyond the session (2026-10-27)

The portable session-closeout Skill now distinguishes temporary working context, the saved episodic record, durable semantic knowledge, and repeatable procedural knowledge. After a confirmed episode save, it can search and draft at most three source-linked candidates in the same authorized vault, skip weak or duplicate candidates, and request separate approval for each additional durable note. It never creates a duplicate episode or treats the session-save approval as permission for extra notes. The canonical Skill and Claude/Codex discovery copies are byte-identical. `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test test/skills.test.mjs` passed 3/3; `git diff --check` passed. The full Windows `npm run test:all` had just passed before this Skill-only change (core 414 total: 408 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips; UI 29/29). Live Claude/Codex workflow and real filing accuracy remain unverified; this is an instruction workflow change, not an automatic classifier.

The independent retrieval evaluator was rerun from clean commit `bb71426ee9d5d4a008f954dc9e89649b4ff80ba9` on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 using the unchanged 30-case fictional fixture (SHA-256 `500e0faffedcdf194c535dc3c0b2f44afe2111814f0d6c034420616392cb5490`). Full and compact response arms both scored 17/30 on the deterministic source/context task proxy, both kept 1.000 source recall and zero privacy violations, while estimated result size was 497 versus 427 tokens (about 14% lower). The machine-readable receipt is `samples/evaluation/reports/retrieval-independent-windows-v3.json`. This reproduces the prior result on current code; it is not a model answer-quality test, provider billing receipt, customer result, or cross-client acceptance.

The MCP server's connection instructions now list only the four memory types enabled for that agent profile. The guide describes their purpose, explains that the model is internal rather than a set of folders, and states that approving a session episode does not approve extra durable notes. The structured guide also returns source, review, and duplicate-episode rules while omitting disabled memory types for scoped agents. Focused MCP guide and Twin memory-policy tests passed 2/2 on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1; `git diff --check` passed. This informs connected clients but does not replace runtime privacy enforcement, which remains enforced by the existing per-tool profile checks.

### Privacy-safe BrainLink response-size observations (2026-10-27)

Successful results from an allowlist of read-only context tools now add a rough serialized-result token estimate to the local activity log. The response text and arguments are not stored; write previews and failed calls are excluded, and estimates are capped at 250,000 tokens. The RSI observer aggregates current/prior 30-day mean estimates alongside failure counts, and the Setup panel renders them in English, Portuguese and Traditional Chinese. This measures only selected BrainLink result payloads, not the full AI prompt, generated answer, or provider billing; averages can shift with the tool mix. Focused telemetry, RSI aggregation, UI, locale and real stdio MCP logging tests passed 5/5; `git diff --check` passed. All examples were synthetic. This is a measured response-size signal for later review, not proof of actual customer token savings or automatic RSI improvement.

### Privacy-safe graph review signals for RSI (2026-10-27)

Successful typed-relationship decisions now add only an allowlisted action label (`approved`, `rejected`, or `reopened`) to the local activity log. The RSI observer reports current and previous 30-day counts in its graph-review category; it never logs or returns note paths, titles, targets, evidence, or relationship text. Counts are feedback signals, not a graph-quality score, and cannot explain why the person acted. Synthetic tests cover action aggregation and canary exclusion; the graph API test verifies approval and rollback events stay content-free. The observer does not change graph extraction, ranking, or permissions.

Full Windows verification on Node.js 24.22.1, commit `0537f10`: `npm run test:all` passed core 419 tests (413 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. The change is pushed to `integration/brainlink-retrieval-evaluation`. These local tests do not replace live Claude/Codex handoff, hosted CI, or customer acceptance.

### Explicit team communication graph suggestions (2026-10-27)

Added the typed predicate `communicates_with` for Person/Team endpoints. Deterministic discovery proposes it when the already-authorized source note contains a supported explicit phrase (`communicates with`, `talks to`, `coordinates with`, or `hands off to`) before a unique same-scope person/team title. The exact sentence is retained as evidence; the edge stays proposed and cannot affect graph retrieval until a person reviews it. Test uses fictional Product and Data teams and verifies the proposal remains unmaterialized. Focused graph, typed-graph and locale tests passed 34/34; `git diff --check` passed. This does not identify entities automatically, resolve arbitrary language, or establish extraction accuracy; the prior full suite predates this change.

### Multilingual typed relationship suggestions (2026-10-27)

Expanded the small deterministic relation phrase set to recognize selected English, Portuguese, and Chinese Traditional/Simplified phrases for workflow inputs/outputs, ownership, dependency, assignment, information sources, and communication. Chinese entity titles use exact substring matching because word boundaries do not apply between Han characters; all candidates still require a unique same-scope entity, an explicit relation phrase, and human review. Focused typed-graph tests passed 15/15, including Portuguese team coordination and a Chinese workflow consuming a named dataset. The full suite on this revision had one unrelated RSI discovery idle-timeout failure (414 passed, 1 failed, 6 skipped); that exact test passed when rerun alone. This is not a clean full-suite result. Phrase coverage is intentionally limited and is not general multilingual extraction; live-vault accuracy remains unverified.

### Better automatic session excerpts and DeepSeek Harness prompt (2026-10-27)

Imported Claude Code and Codex summaries now preserve the first user goal, the latest meaningful user direction, and the final assistant reply in separate size limits. Low-signal confirmations such as “continue” are skipped when choosing the recent direction; secret redaction is applied again while composing the excerpt. The last reply is explicitly marked unverified. This remains a deterministic excerpt, not AI-generated or verified outcome understanding. `test/sessions.test.mjs` passed 13/13; combined session and backup tests passed 29 with one Windows symlink-privilege skip. Full Windows Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed: core 424 tests (418 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. The full run exposed a flaky 600 ms idle timeout in the scheduled-backup integration fixture; its setup timeout is now 10 minutes because that test does not cover idle exit. Dedicated idle-exit tests still pass in the same full run. DeepSeek Harness is recorded as a requested, unverified client, and `docs/DEEPSEEK-HARNESS-SECONDBRAIN-CONNECTION-PROMPT.md` provides a ready-to-paste discovery and setup prompt. The prompt does not connect the Harness; its MCP capability, runtime location, real authorization and live BrainLink call remain unverified.

### Cancel idle shutdown when Setup receives new activity (2026-10-27)

Fixed an idle-exit race: a request arriving during the 100 ms shutdown grace period now cancels the pending close, as well as restarting the idle timer. Added a regression test that waits until the grace period, sends a health request, verifies the server stays available, and then verifies normal idle exit resumes. RSI discovery lifecycle file passed 20/20; the two idle lifecycle checks also passed in three repeated runs (6/6); `git diff --check` passed. The subsequent full Windows suite passed on commit `15c7417` (core 418 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips; UI 29/29); see the latest full-suite receipt above.

Full Windows verification attempt on `e68baf6`: core 422 tests (415 passed, 1 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips); the idle-exit regression passed in the full run. The single failure was the synthetic RSI monitoring evidence secret scan; `test/rsi-ledger.test.mjs` passed 9/9 when rerun alone. UI suite passed 29/29. The failure cause has not been established, so the full suite is not marked clean.

The intermittent monitoring check was traced to the generated `monitoring_id`: its date and random hexadecimal suffix could look like a card/account number to the privacy scanner when the suffix began with enough digits. The ID now inserts a fixed letter between those parts; the scanner continues checking all submitted evidence. The subsequent full Windows suite passed on commit `15c7417` (core 418 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips; UI 29/29); see the latest full-suite receipt above.

### DeepSeek Harness profile-scoped connections (2026-10-27)

BrainLink now discovers named DeepSeek Harness profiles under `$DSH_HOME/profiles/<name>` and writes one MCP entry into each profile's own `cordis.patch.yml`. Each connection gets a different BrainLink agent identity/token and a distinct MCP server name, so each profile can receive its own Twin permissions. Setup health checks, token reads and disconnects resolve the same profile-specific file. When a legacy home-level BrainLink entry is present alongside named profiles, the UI exposes it for disconnect but will not reconnect it; attempting a named-profile connection is blocked until that shared entry is removed, because Harness applies the home patch to every profile. Legacy detection also finds renamed BrainLink entries, and disconnect preserves unrelated home-level plugins. The existing root-level connector remains available if no named profiles exist. Token-bearing config files, temporary writes, and backups receive a Windows ACL limited to the current user, SYSTEM, and Administrators; Unix files use mode `0600`. Harness's official CLI and MCP-client docs describe per-profile patch files and the shared home-level layer: https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/apps/cli/README.md and https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/mcp/mcp-client/README.md. Tests cover independent profile tokens/files, disconnect isolation, renamed shared-token cleanup, and actual Windows ACL entries for both connection and disconnect backups. Windows Twin-profile and connector/Setup suites passed 46/46; the end-to-end Setup test confirms Twin token hashing, permission assignment, and disconnect revocation. UI suite passed 29/29. Live Harness profile discovery, actual model tool calls, and Unix mode assertions remain unverified. Rollback: revert the profile resolver, permission helper, UI hint, locale, tests, and this status entry; no data migration is required.

### Bounded graph-wide relationship discovery (2026-10-27)

The 3D graph now has an on-demand scan across the caller's enabled, non-private vaults. Each request checks at most 20 source notes and returns deterministic same-scope text candidates; the UI can continue through batches. A candidate must be explicitly saved, then separately approved in the existing review queue before it appears as a graph edge. The scan reuses the existing candidate detector, current vault filters and proposal endpoint. Added HTTP coverage for public/private isolation, client-vault exclusion, invalid paging, and proposal-only behavior, plus UI/locale checks. Windows graph suite: 19/19 passed. The first attempted `npm test -- --test-name-pattern=...` invoked the package's full serial suite rather than a focused run and was interrupted after many unrelated passing tests; it is not counted as a full-suite result. Focused repeat command: `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/graph3d.test.mjs`. Cross-client and hosted CI validation remain unverified. Rollback: revert this feature's graph helper, authenticated route, graph UI, translations, test and status entry; no data migration is needed.

### Graph discovery review fixes (2026-10-27)

An independent review found three UX/performance edge cases in graph discovery. Read-only results now explain why they cannot be saved; follow-up pages carry a scan fingerprint over visible source paths, modification times and sizes, and a changed vault invalidates the page sequence with a restart message; the relationship detector builds its whole-vault entity index once per batch rather than once per source note. Tests cover read-only save eligibility, mutation between pages, and batch/single-source result parity. Windows graph plus typed-graph suites passed 34/34. `git diff --check` and locale JSON validation passed. This reduces repeated indexing but has not yet been benchmarked on a production-size vault; hosted CI and cross-client UI checks remain unverified. Rollback: revert the batching helper, scan fingerprint, read-only UI state, tests and status entry; no data migration is required.

### CLI stdio full-suite timeout fix (2026-10-27)

The 1.2.8 full core suite had one intermittent failure in the `brainlink serve` subprocess test: under serial-suite load it could receive fewer than three replies within the old five-second window, then dereference a missing response. The test now allows 15 seconds, stops waiting if the child exits, and reports the response count and exit code clearly. The focused test passed 4/4 consecutive runs after the change. This improves the test's startup tolerance; the complete 436-test core suite has not yet been rerun, so the prior 429/1/6 receipt remains the latest full-run result.

Added the `organize-sources` MCP prompt for clients that support prompts. It accepts one explicit vault and existing folder, limits a run to five queue candidates, reads and searches only in that vault, previews each note, requires separate user approval before saving, and records a receipt only after a confirmed save. Scoped agents see it only when they have all required read, search, proposal, and receipt grants; direct `propose_note` calls now correctly enforce the same explicit per-vault proposal grant. The server now uses the same memory mapping during Twin create, edit, and built-in-app connection: `propose_note` requires semantic memory, and only `read_session` requires episodic memory. A profile with proposal access but no search permission can still pass the connection check through its advertised MCP tool list; this check makes no vault write. Research candidates must now match both the user's problem signal and the topic used for the public-source search, in addition to a BrainLink workflow term. The rule is recorded as `lexical-topic-gate-v2`; Chinese topic terms use the existing overlapping-bigram matcher. An offline fixture covers 28 benign fictional examples and two hostile examples. Current score: 12 true positives, 0 false positives, 0 false negatives and 16 true negatives; both hostile items are blocked. Fixture SHA-256: `c443ba9359b4c10c9a298b4966c5c7ace92a67b552ccf23171aad48a44bacdb0`. The three known false positives informed the new topic labels, so this is a development regression check, not an independent or held-out result. Focused discovery, evaluator and ledger tests passed 40/40 on Windows with Node.js 24.22.1; `npm run evaluate:rsi-discovery` and `git diff --check` passed. The later full-suite entry records the completed suite for this implementation; a separate unseen set remains pending. This does not prove broad research relevance or autonomous self-improvement.

### Show RSI topic-match evidence in proposals (2026-10-28)

New discoveries retain the exact topic terms that passed the lexical gate. Setup now displays those terms next to the problem and workflow evidence. The field is optional when reading older proposals, preserving their existing ledger records. Focused discovery, ledger, evaluator, locale and UI checks passed 53/53; the full suite passed with 477 core passes, 6 platform skips and 29/29 UI tests. No real AI-client call is claimed.

### Make the scheduled-scan retry test deterministic (2026-10-28)

The full suite exposed a timing-sensitive assertion: a 500 ms retry delay could expire several times before a heavily loaded runner observed the first failed scan. The production retry behavior was unchanged. The fixture now gives the retry a long future deadline, then advances its temporary config timestamp after confirming the first failure and recreating the fictional source folder. The focused ingestion schedule suite passed 4/4, then `npm run test:all` passed on commit `476d636cb7d5b5a65d161f782aae7f213d680a2e`: core 483 total (477 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29 on Windows with Node.js 24.22.1. No real vault data was used.
### Windows backup recovery-key ACL hardening (2026-10-28)

BrainLink now restricts the Windows recovery-key file directly, rather than relying on inherited Local AppData permissions. It disables inheritance, removes allow grants for Everyone, Authenticated Users, and BUILTIN Users, then grants full control to the current user, SYSTEM, and Administrators. Key creation applies the ACL before publishing the file; reading an existing key reapplies the policy and fails closed if Windows cannot enforce it. A Windows regression test plants an Everyone read grant on a synthetic key in a temporary directory and verifies BrainLink removes it while retaining current-user access. This is local host regression evidence, not an independent Windows security audit; same-user processes remain able to read the key. The focused backup-key test passed 4/4 on Windows, including ACL repair. On this Windows host with Node.js 24.22.1, `npm run test:all` passed: core 495 total (489 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. The skips include Windows symlink-privilege cases. `git diff --check` and Node syntax checks passed. Rollback: revert the ACL helper, its regression test, and this note; no migration is required because ACLs are repaired whenever the key is read.

### DeepSeek Harness read-only session import (2026-10-28)

Added an incremental DeepSeek Harness session importer to the standard BrainLink refresh path. It reads only `DSH_HOME/sessions` (or `~/.dsh/sessions`), accepts raw JSONL and concatenated Zstandard JSONL for v3/v4 headers, and enforces 32 MiB compressed/input and 128 MiB decoded limits. It imports only direct user prompts and visible assistant text; injected context, system/developer messages, reasoning, tools and other events are excluded. Secret redaction runs before storage and every imported session is private. Unsupported versions and malformed files do not advance their cursor and are retried. No Harness source file is changed.

On Windows with Node.js 24.22.1, `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test --test-concurrency=1 test/sessions.test.mjs test/wiring.test.mjs` passed 47/47 in 203.3 seconds. The full `npm run test:all` run also passed on this source: core 499 tests (493 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips) and UI 29/29. Synthetic checks cover concatenated Zstandard frames, raw v3, v4, incremental refresh, redaction, excluded-content canaries, private-by-default storage, and discovery through both `brainlink sync` and MCP session listing. `git diff --check` and Node syntax checks passed. The installed DeepSeek Harness is not present, so no real user session or live-client import was tested. This does not add a Harness end-of-task signal; imported sessions may be active and still need reviewed closeout. See [SESSION-FILING.md](SESSION-FILING.md).

### Prepare private Harness closeout drafts after an idle period (2026-10-28)

When local AI summaries and automatic drafting are explicitly enabled, BrainLink now waits 30 minutes after a Harness completed-turn cue, requires a valid latest visible-message timestamp no later than the cue, and rechecks both the cue and imported session after local inference. A later turn cancels the draft. The output stays private, is marked unverified, and still requires preview and user confirmation before filing. A newer idle cue replaces an older draft for the same session. Setup wording explains this heuristic in English, Traditional Chinese and Portuguese. Focused local checks passed: 15/15 across `test/local-summary-api.test.mjs`, `test/session-end-cue.test.mjs` and `test/local-summary-ui.test.mjs`; JavaScript syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed. ### Opt-in automatic filing for local session closeouts (2026-10-28)

Local session closeout now has a separate **Automatically save the private draft to the saved project folder** control. It is off by default and is only active when local summaries and auto-drafting are also enabled. The background worker writes only to the exact project route previously saved by the user, after confirming that the destination vault is writable and the folder still exists. It never guesses a folder or falls back to intake. Before writing, it checks that exact folder for a private `episode` note with the same BrainLink session source, preventing duplicate notes on later scheduler ticks and after Setup restarts. Filed notes remain `status: proposed`, `confidence: reported`, and private; generated text is explicitly unverified. If the exact route is missing or file creation fails, the draft stays in private BrainLink app data and the Sessions screen reports the state. The cue endpoint rechecks the saved note so the UI still shows it as filed after an app restart.

The implementation reuses the existing local closeout, destination routing, `proposeNote` writer, and correction/withdrawal workflow. Tests use fictional projects and a fake local model. They verify exact-route filing, no guessed destination, private/unverified metadata, duplicate prevention, and persisted UI status. Focused Windows checks passed: local summary API/UI, filing, config, and cue tests passed 20/20; the relevant session-end-cue/API/UI set passed 19/19; `npm run test:ui` passed 29/29; `git diff --check` and JavaScript syntax checks passed. A full `npm run test:all` run completed core tests with 518 passes, 1 failure, and 6 platform/privilege skips. The single failure was an existing RSI ledger API test whose earlier update response lacked its expected object; rerunning that test alone passed 1/1. This intermittent suite-level failure remains unresolved, so this change is not claimed to have a clean full-suite pass. No live Ollama inference, real desktop-client lifecycle, hosted CI, or customer acceptance was performed.

### Enforce typed graph subject and target rules (2026-10-30)

Added per-predicate subject and target type checks to typed relationship discovery, direct AI note proposals, manual graph suggestions, review queues, and verified-edge materialization. This prevents a team note that mentions its workflow consuming data from incorrectly creating a team-to-dataset `consumes` edge. Discovery also stops matching a later relationship verb to a target that belongs to that later clause (for example, `consumes` must not attach to the output after `produces`). Invalid existing YAML is left untouched and excluded from graph retrieval/review until corrected; the source note is not automatically migrated or deleted. The documented current type matrix remains local product code and has not yet been validated with customer workflow ontologies.

### Serialize concurrent graph edits (2026-10-30)

Graph note saves and typed-relationship review/proposal actions are now queued per vault and note inside the Setup process. This closes a same-process race where two requests could both validate the same old ETag, then overwrite each other's edit. The queued second request re-reads the current note and receives the existing stale-revision error; edits to different notes remain independent. External editors/processes are not covered by this in-process queue and remain protected by the existing revision check only.

Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 verification: the graph suite passed 20/20, including a new simultaneous-save regression proving exactly one request succeeds and the other must reload. GitHub Actions remains blocked before runner startup by the repository account billing limit, so these are local test results; hosted CI and desktop acceptance remain unverified.
# Signed update check UI and local verification (2026-10-30)

Added a Settings action that checks one fixed GitHub release-manifest URL. The backend requires the Setup session token, applies a 12-second timeout and bounded response read, allows only a few HTTPS redirects to approved GitHub release hosts, checks the content type, verifies the Ed25519 signature using the compiled publisher trust key, and returns only the matching signed release metadata. A separate user action streams the selected package into a temporary directory and verifies the exact signed size and SHA-256 before exposing it through a one-time, five-minute, locally authenticated download link. The UI clearly distinguishes an available signed release from a build where no publisher key is configured. The publisher key remains intentionally empty; ordinary builds make no update network request until the release owner pins a public key. Packages are never installed or launched automatically. See `docs/UPDATE-SYSTEM.md` for the enablement and release steps.

Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 verification: full `npm run test:all` passed — core 568 total (562 passed, 0 failed, 6 platform/privilege skips), UI 43/43. The focused update client, authenticated Setup route, verified download, signature, signing CLI and locale tests passed 18/18. The API fixture proved a bad token cannot consume a download ticket, valid package bytes are returned with attachment headers, the ticket works once, and the staged file is removed after serving. A separate package-byte mismatch test verified that failed staging is deleted. `npm run smoke:installed:win` on the final download implementation built the 1.2.8 installer, installed and launched it through the bundled runtime, passed Setup health/UI checks, then uninstalled while preserving isolated user data and restoring PATH. `git diff --check` passed. Tests use temporary signing keys and fake release responses. They prove local verification logic and Windows packaging, not a live signed GitHub release, key provisioning, Windows temp-folder ACL review, installer handoff, key rotation, hosted CI, or release readiness.

### Generate signed-update payloads from built release packages (2026-10-30)

Added `npm run updates:create-payload -- --dist dist --out brainlink-update-payload.json --channel stable` to calculate release metadata from the exact Windows installer, universal macOS package, and Linux x64/arm64 Debian and tar.gz packages. The builder requires all expected files for the current version, rejects missing, linked, non-file, oversized, or changing packages, calculates streaming SHA-256 and exact sizes, uses the verifier's fixed GitHub release URL format, and refuses to overwrite an existing reviewed payload. Hosted cross-platform CI, publisher-key provisioning, signed release, cross-client user acceptance, and commercial release readiness remain unverified.

### Reject normalized invalid dates in signed update metadata (2026-10-30)

The update manifest verifier and local payload builder now reject impossible UTC dates and times instead of accepting JavaScript's normalized date values (for example, February 31). The payload-to-signature test confirms generated metadata still passes the exact shipped verifier. Windows/Node.js 24.22.1 `npm run test:all` passed on the worktree based on `a10eeb1` with this change included: core 573 total (566 passed, 0 failed, 7 platform/privilege skips), UI 44/44. Focused update manifest, payload and signing tests passed 12/13 with 1 Windows symlink-privilege skip; syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed. This is local evidence; hosted CI remains blocked by the account billing gate.

### Hermes CLI session-finalize reminder (2026-10-30)

BrainLink can now add or remove an opt-in Hermes `on_session_finalize` shell hook for the exact profile selected for session import. The hook creates only a local reminder with an opaque profile-scoped session reference and timestamp. It rejects non-CLI events, stores no raw session ID, transcript, working directory, or hook payload, and links only to one matching imported session. The YAML update is backed up, preserves unrelated settings/hooks, and fails closed on malformed YAML. Setup discloses the shell hook's user-level permissions and asks for confirmation before enabling; Hermes may separately require its own first-use trust approval. Hermes import behavior is unchanged.

Evidence on Windows/Node.js 24.22.1: `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test test/session-hooks.test.mjs` passed 5/5; `node --import ./test/_preload.mjs --test test/session-end-cue.test.mjs test/sessions.test.mjs` passed 27/27; final full `npm test` passed 568, failed 0, skipped 7 (Windows symlink/privilege skips); `npm run test:ui` passed 44/44; `git diff --check` passed. Hermes Agent v0.21.0's actual `hermes hooks test on_session_finalize` ran the generated command in an isolated temporary profile and wrote an opaque cue. No real Hermes config was changed. This does not prove a real CLI teardown or consent prompt, nor Hermes Desktop/TUI/gateway behavior, Claude-to-Hermes cross-client continuation, hosted CI, or release readiness. The first full core run had one unrelated RSI Setup test failure; that test passed alone and the complete repeat then passed. GitHub Actions cannot start while the repository account's billing/spending limit blocks runners.

It also checks that the caller's `HERMES_HOME` is restored. `test/packaging-runtime.test.mjs` and the Hermes hook suite passed 7/7, and PowerShell parsed the updated smoke script without errors. The end-to-end installer run was not performed: automatic command review rejected the build/install/uninstall invocation, which would rebuild `build/windows` and run the temporary Windows installer. This result is not evidence that the installed package contains or runs the Hermes controls.

The skill installer rejected valid `SKILL.md` frontmatter when a Windows Git checkout used CRLF. A regression test converts all four bundled skills to CRLF, then installs, inspects, and removes them while checking that each copied file remains byte-identical. A separate test cleanup removed a hard-coded calendar date so the long Windows test suite can cross local midnight safely.

Linux x64 and ARM64 package builds completed and all four SHA-256 sidecars verified; the x64 Debian and portable packages passed isolated install, version, fictional-vault initialization, uninstall, and settings-preservation checks. The Windows `npm run smoke:installed:win` test also passed: bundled-runtime launch, shortcuts, authenticated Setup, temporary Hermes hook controls, isolated uninstall, and preservation of user data and PATH. No user vault or AI-client config was used.

This change fixes metadata parsing and does not expand permissions. ARM64 was not executed on ARM hardware, hosted GitHub Actions remains blocked by the account billing/spending limit, and live cross-client/customer acceptance remains incomplete.
